# Luma AMD Speedrun: Phase 2 — VLIW-Informed Custom Kernel Approaches

## Context

**Phase 1 (parameter tuning) is complete.** All aiter API parameters have been exhausted:
- `doweight_stage1=True`: correctness failure (SiLU nonlinearity)
- `block_size_M=64`: GPU memory fault on benchmark shapes
- `dispatch_policy=1`: 20-79% slower
- `OPUS sorting`: no effect on MXFP4 variant
- `fast_mode=True` (MLA): 17-21% slower
- `num_kv_splits` sweep (MLA): all tested values slower

Phase 2 applies lessons from the Anthropic VLIW take-home challenge
(`research/challenges/anthropic_challenge/`) to pursue custom kernel approaches.

| MoE | ~185 us (ref match) | 145 us | 1.28x | `amd-moe-mxfp4` |
| MLA | ~191 us (ref match) | 4.3 us | 44x | `amd-mixed-mla` |

**Priority:** GEMM JIT bypass (highest confidence) → MoE CK exploration → MLA MXFP4 KV test

## Implementation Tasks

### Task 1: GEMM — `get_torch_quant` JIT bypass (target: match ref ~24 us, then optimize)

**Objective:** Replace `ref_kernel` delegation with our own quantize-then-GEMM pipeline using
`get_torch_quant` (pure PyTorch, no Triton JIT) to avoid the call-site correctness bug.

**Key insight:** MoE `reference.py:109` already calls `get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)`
successfully. The GEMM `reference.py:95` uses `get_triton_quant` which has the call-site bug.
If `get_torch_quant` with `shuffle=True` produces compatible output for `gemm_a4w4`, we bypass
the JIT issue entirely.

**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`

**Implementation (attempt 1 — torch_quant with shuffle):**
```python
import aiter
from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
from task import input_t, output_t

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    A = A.contiguous()
    quant_func = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    return aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale, B_scale_sh,
                           dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
```

**Fallback (attempt 2 — manual shuffle):**
```python
from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
from aiter.utility import fp4_utils

quant_func = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A)  # no shuffle kwarg
A_q_shuffled = shuffle_weight(A_q.view(m, k // 2), layout=(16, 16))
A_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(A_scale)
```

**Fallback (attempt 3 — hip_quant):**
```python
quant_func = aiter.get_hip_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
```

**Verify:** `--mode test` → 0.0 max error → `--mode benchmark` → `--mode leaderboard`

### Task 3: MoE — Remote aiter introspection (0 risk, high info value)

**Objective:** Submit a test script that prints aiter internal state to understand
what CK kernel configs, tile sizes, and CSV tuning data are available. This uses
Popcorn's stderr output to dump diagnostic information.

**Files:** `kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`

**Implementation:**
```python
import sys, os
from task import input_t, output_t
from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe, get_2stage_cfgs
from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    (hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
     gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
     gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
     gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
     topk_weights, topk_ids, config) = data

    # Dump aiter CK kernel selection info to stderr
    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
    E = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
    print(f"INTROSPECT: M={M}, E={E}, config={config}", file=sys.stderr)

    # Try to read tuned CSV configs
    import aiter
    aiter_path = os.path.dirname(aiter.__file__)
    csv_path = os.path.join(aiter_path, "configs")
    if os.path.isdir(csv_path):
        for f in sorted(os.listdir(csv_path)):
            fpath = os.path.join(csv_path, f)
            if f.endswith('.csv'):
                print(f"CSV: {f}", file=sys.stderr)
                with open(fpath) as fh:
                    for i, line in enumerate(fh):
                        if i < 5:  # first 5 lines (header + samples)
                            print(f"  {line.rstrip()}", file=sys.stderr)

    # Still return correct output (delegate to reference)
    from reference import ref_kernel
    return ref_kernel(data)
```

**Verify:** `--mode test` → read STDERR output for CSV configs and shape info.
No risk to correctness since we delegate to `ref_kernel`.

### Task 4: MoE — Direct CK stage kernel call (bypass fused_moe dispatch)

**Depends on:** Task 3 (need CK kernel names and configs)

**Objective:** VLIW "Two Builders" pattern. Build a custom MoE pipeline that calls
aiter's CK 2-stage kernels (`ck_moe_stage1`, `ck_moe_stage2`) directly with
hand-tuned parameters, bypassing `fused_moe`'s auto-dispatch.

**Key observation from prior stderr logs:** The CK kernels selected are:
- Stage 1: `moe_ck2stages_gemm1_64x32x32x128_1x1_MulABScaleShuffled_v3_...`
- Stage 2: `REDACTED...`

The tile is `64x32x32x128`. If we can call these directly with different tile configs
(e.g., the stage2 kernel with a different N_swizzle), we may improve throughput.

**Files:** `kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`

```python
    # 1. Sort tokens to experts (same as reference)

    # 3. Call ck_moe_stage2 directly with custom params
    # 4. Scatter results back
    ...
```

**Risk:** High — these internal functions may not be importable or may have
undocumented requirements. Fallback is to continue using `fused_moe` with defaults.

**Verify:** `--mode test` → correctness → `--mode benchmark` for timing comparison

### Task 5: MLA — Try MXFP4 KV cache (input already provides it)

**Objective:** The input `kv_data` dict provides three KV cache formats:
- `"bf16"`: Tensor (total_kv, 1, 576) — highest precision
- `"fp8"`: (Tensor, Tensor) — current reference uses this
- `"mxfp4"`: (Tensor, Tensor) — fp4x2 (total_kv, 1, 288) + scale

We've only tested fp8. MXFP4 has 2x less memory bandwidth (4-bit vs 8-bit),
which could significantly speed up the memory-bound decode kernel. The question
is whether `mla_decode_fwd` supports MXFP4 KV, or if we need a different kernel path.

**Files:** `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`

**Implementation:**
```python
# Test if mla_decode_fwd accepts fp4x2 dtype KV buffer
kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = kv_data["mxfp4"]
# Need to check: does mla_decode_fwd support fp4x2 dtype?
# If not, we need aiter's MXFP4-specific MLA kernel (if it exists)
```

**Discovery step:** Submit a version that prints `type(kv_buffer_mxfp4.dtype)` and
tries to call `mla_decode_fwd` with it. The error message will reveal whether MXFP4
is supported.

**Verify:** `--mode test` → if correctness passes, `--mode benchmark` for timing

---

### Task 6: Deep source reading — aiter tuned CSV dump

**Objective:** Extract the full auto-tuning CSV configurations from the remote MI355X
environment. These CSVs drive the CK kernel selection in `fused_moe` and potentially
`gemm_a4w4`. Understanding them reveals what tile configs exist for each shape.

**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` (any kernel will do)

**Implementation:** Same pattern as Task 3 — submit a script that reads and prints
the aiter configs directory, then delegates to ref_kernel.

```python
import os, sys, aiter
from reference import ref_kernel
from task import input_t, output_t

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    aiter_path = os.path.dirname(aiter.__file__)
    # Print all CSV content (not just headers)
    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(os.path.join(aiter_path, "configs")):
        for f in sorted(files):
            if f.endswith('.csv'):
                fpath = os.path.join(root, f)
                print(f"\n=== {f} ===", file=sys.stderr)
                with open(fpath) as fh:
                    print(fh.read(), file=sys.stderr)
    return ref_kernel(data)
```

**Verify:** `--mode test` → read STDERR for full CSV contents

## Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|------|------------|
| `get_torch_quant` shuffle produces different layout than `get_triton_quant` | Fallback: manual `shuffle_weight()` + `e8m0_shuffle()`, then hip_quant |
| `gemm_afp4wfp4` not importable or different API | Discovery submission first to print module contents |
| CK stage kernels not directly importable from `aiter.fused_moe` | Task 3 introspection reveals what's importable |
| `mla_decode_fwd` doesn't support MXFP4 KV dtype | Error message reveals this; fallback to fp8 (current) |
| CSV configs empty or not useful | Low risk — we know they exist from JIT build logs |
| Slow iteration (~2-10 min/submit) | Parallel first batch (Tasks 1,3,6); analyze before Task 2,4,5 |

## Verification

For each task:
1. `--mode test` must pass with 0.0 (or < tolerance) max error
2. `--mode benchmark` timing compared against current baselines (GEMM 24us, MoE 185us, MLA 191us)
3. Only submit `--mode leaderboard` if benchmark shows improvement over current ranking
4. Read STDERR output for all introspection tasks to extract aiter internal configs

```
     4→Key discovery: Optimal KSPLIT depends on BOTH estimated_m AND num_experts.
     5→For large expert counts (257E, dexp=256), KSPLIT=4 is better at estimated_m≈4
     6→because smaller expert weight matrices benefit more from K-dimension splitting.
     7→For small expert counts (33E, dexp=512+), KSPLIT=2 remains optimal for sparse.
     8→
     9→Phase 9 benchmark data (vs Phase 8 KSPLIT=2 baseline):
    10→  bs=16,  257E (em=0): KSPLIT=4 → 91.3 vs 95.1 µs  (KSPLIT=4 better, -4%)
    11→  bs=128, 257E (em=4): KSPLIT=4 → 172  vs 186  µs  (KSPLIT=4 better, -7.5%)
    12→  bs=512, 257E (em=17): KSPLIT=4 → 284 vs 275  µs  (KSPLIT=2 better, +3.3%)
    13→  bs=16,  33E  (em=4): KSPLIT=4 → 61.4 vs 59.8 µs  (KSPLIT=2 better, +2.7%)
    14→
    15→Strategy:
    16→  - num_experts >= 200 AND estimated_m < 10: KSPLIT=4 (large expert count, sparse)
    17→  - estimated_m < 50 (others): KSPLIT=2 (moderate sparse, or small expert count)
    18→  - estimated_m >= 50: KSPLIT=0 (dense, CK MXFP4 path)
    19→  Always BYPASS_TUNE for sparse shapes to override CSV-locked 257E configs.
    20→"""

    22→from task import input_t, output_t
    23→from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
    24→from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
    25→
    26→
    27→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    28→    (
    29→        hidden_states,
    30→        gate_up_weight,
    31→        down_weight,
    32→        gate_up_weight_scale,
    33→        down_weight_scale,
    34→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
    35→        down_weight_shuffled,
    36→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
    37→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    38→        topk_weights,
    39→        topk_ids,
    40→        config,
    41→    ) = data
    42→
    43→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
    44→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
    45→
    46→    num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
    47→    estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts
    48→
    49→    if estimated_m >= 50:
    50→        # Dense: CK MXFP4-optimized path (ksplit=0).
    51→        os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
    52→        os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
    53→    elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
    54→        # Large expert count, very sparse: split_k=4 exploits K-parallelism.
    55→        # Smaller expert weights (dexp≈256) benefit more from higher splits.
    56→        os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
    57→        os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "4"
    58→    else:
    59→        # Sparse (few experts or moderate token density): cktile split_k=2.
    60→        os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
    61→        os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"
```

```
     2→MLA decode — hybrid: torch-native for small workloads, aiter for large.
     3→
     4→Crossover analysis from benchmarking on MI355X:
     5→  Small (bs * kv <= 400k tokens): torch.einsum beats aiter 3-stage pipeline
     6→    - bs=4, kv=1k (4k tokens):    26 µs vs ~150 µs  (5.7x faster)
     7→    - bs=4, kv=8k (33k tokens):   37 µs vs ~169 µs  (4.5x faster)

    10→    - bs=64, kv=1k (65k tokens):  64 µs vs ~175 µs  (2.7x faster)
    11→    - bs=256, kv=1k (262k tokens): 175 µs vs ~196 µs (1.1x faster)
    12→  Large (bs * kv > 400k tokens): fp8 ASM kernel wins due to bandwidth
    13→    - bs=64, kv=8k (524k tokens): ~241 µs vs our 280 µs
    14→    - bs=256, kv=8k (2M tokens):  ~383 µs vs our 964 µs
    15→
    16→Geomean: ~97 µs vs ~199 µs baseline (2x improvement over ref_kernel).
    17→"""
    18→import torch
    19→from reference import ref_kernel
    20→from task import input_t, output_t
    21→
    22→
    23→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)
    24→
    25→# Crossover: torch-native faster below this, aiter faster above
    26→TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000  # tokens (bs * kvseqlen)
    27→
    28→
    29→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    30→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    31→
    32→    bs = config["batch_size"]
    33→    nq = config["num_heads"]
    34→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
    35→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]  # 512
    36→
    37→    # Detect qseqlen — all benchmark shapes have qseqlen=1 (decode)
    38→    total_q = q.shape[0]
    39→    qseqlen = total_q // bs
    40→
    41→    # Use aiter for: non-decode shapes, or large workloads (bandwidth-bound)
    42→    if qseqlen != 1 or bs * kvseqlen > TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD:
    43→        return ref_kernel(data)
    44→
    45→    # Small decode: torch-native bf16 attention (bypasses aiter 3-stage pipeline)
    46→    # Avoids: metadata computation + split-K ASM launch + parallel reduce
    47→    kv = kv_data["bf16"]  # (total_kv, 1, 576)
    48→    kv = kv.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)  # (bs, kvseqlen, 576)
    49→
    50→    # Q: (bs, nq, 576) for decode (qseqlen=1 → total_q=bs)
    51→    scores = torch.einsum("bnh,bsh->bns", q, kv) * SM_SCALE
    52→    weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)  # (bs, nq, kvseqlen)
    53→
    54→    v = kv[:, :, :dv]  # (bs, kvseqlen, 512)
    55→    out = torch.einsum("bns,bsd->bnd", weights, v)  # (bs, nq, 512)
    56→
    57→    return out
    58→
```

```
     1→"""
     2→FP4 quant + FP4 GEMM reference: bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> bf16 C.
     3→Quant logic follows aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.py (get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)).
     4→"""
     5→import torch
     6→from task import input_t, output_t
     7→from utils import make_match_reference
     8→from aiter import QuantType,dtypes
     9→import aiter
    10→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    11→# K must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)
    12→SCALE_GROUP_SIZE = 32
    13→
    14→def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):# -> input_t:
    15→    """
    16→    Generate random bf16 inputs A [m, k], B [n, k] and quantized MXFP4 B, shuffled B and B_scale.
    17→
    18→    Returns:
    19→        Tuple of (A, B), both bf16 on cuda.
    20→    """
    21→    assert k % 64 == 0, "k must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)"
    22→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    23→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    24→    A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    25→    B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    26→    
    27→    # quantized mxfp4 B
    28→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    29→    B_q, B_scale_sh = quant_func(B, shuffle=True)
    30→    
    31→    # shuffle B(weight) to (16,16) tile coalesced
    32→    B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
    33→    return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
    34→
    35→def run_torch_fp4_mm(
    36→    x: torch.Tensor,
    37→    w: torch.Tensor,
    38→    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
    39→    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
    40→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
    41→) -> torch.Tensor:
    42→    """
    43→    PyTorch reference: dequant MXFP4 + E8M0 scale -> f32 -> mm -> dtype.
    44→    Same logic as aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.run_torch.
    45→    x: [m, k//2] fp4 packed, w: [n, k//2] fp4 packed
    46→    x_scales: [m, k//32] E8M0, w_scales: [n, k//32] E8M0
    47→    Returns: [m, n] in dtype
    48→    """
    49→    from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    50→
    51→    m, _ = x.shape
    52→    n, _ = w.shape
    53→    # fp4 packed -> f32
    54→    x_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(x)
    55→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(w)
    56→    # E8M0 scale: [*, k//32] -> repeat 32 along k -> f32
    57→    x_scales = x_scales[:m].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    58→    x_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(x_scales)
    59→    x_f32 = x_f32 * x_scales_f32
    60→    w_scales = w_scales[:n].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    61→    w_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(w_scales)
    62→    w_f32 = w_f32 * w_scales_f32
    63→    return torch.mm(x_f32, w_f32.T).to(dtype)[:m, :n]
    64→
    65→
    66→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    67→    """
    68→    Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given.
    69→    Returns gemm_a4w4 for check_implementation.
    70→    """
    71→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    72→    A = A.contiguous()
    73→    B = B.contiguous()
    74→    m, k = A.shape
    75→    n, _ = B.shape
    76→    
    77→    # 1) PyTorch impl just for your reference: dequant fp4 + e8m0 -> f32 -> mm -> bf16
    78→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    79→    # quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    80→    # quant_func(x, shuffle=False) -> (dtypes.fp4x2, scale); scale layout matches gemm_a4w4
    81→    # A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=False)
    82→    # B_q, B_scale = quant_func(B, shuffle=False)
    83→
    84→    # gemm_a4w4 expects A [M,K/2], B [N,K/2] as dtypes.fp4x2; A_scale/B_scale [*,K/32] E8M0
    85→    # quant_func returns scale as dtypes.fp8_e8m0; gemm_a4w4 accepts E8M0, no view to uint8 needed
    86→    # slice to exact shapes [m,k_scale] / [n,k_scale] (quant may return padded scale)
    87→    
    88→    # k_scale = k // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    89→    # A_scale = A_scale[:m, :k_scale].contiguous()
    90→    # B_scale = B_scale[:n, :k_scale].contiguous()
    91→    # out_torch = run_torch_fp4_mm(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, torch.bfloat16)
    92→
    93→    # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path: needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)
    94→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    95→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    96→    A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    97→    # to be noted, aiter also has other a4w4 implements using triton, https://github.com/ROCm/aiter/blob/main/aiter/ops/triton/gemm/basic/gemm_afp4wfp4.py
    98→    out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
    99→        A_q,
   100→        B_shuffle,
   101→        A_scale_sh,
   102→        B_scale_sh,
   103→        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
   104→        bpreshuffle=True,
   105→    )
   106→    return out_gemm
   107→
   108→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Key decisions from skills analysis before writing any code:**

1. **Task 1 (get_torch_quant) is already solved** — The current GEMM submission already uses `dynamic_mxfp4_quant`, which is the patched Triton kernel (#975). All three `get_*_quant` backends fail. The plan's Task 1 approach would fail; our current code is the correct fix.

2. **Task 5 (MLA MXFP4 KV) is blocked at the hardware level** — The ASM kernel asserts `head_size == KV.size(3)`, but MXFP4 packs 2 values per byte (576→288). No workaround exists in the current aiter version.

3. **Task 2 (gemm_afp4wfp4) has a known limitation** — It rejects native `torch.float4_e2m1fn_x2` dtype with a `KeyError`. Need to `.view(torch.uint8)` all tensors. But even with that fix, the Triton kernel may interpret bytes differently than CK's `gemm_a4w4`.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Now let me start executing. I'll begin with the **parallel zero-risk probes** (Tasks 2, 3, and the GEMM afp4wfp4 attempt) — but since Popcorn submissions are serial, I'll write all the submission files first, then submit them one at a time.

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"MoE introspection probe — dump aiter CK kernel internals to stderr.\"\"\"\nimport sys\nimport os\nimport inspect\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    (\n        hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights, topk_ids, config,\n    ) = data\n\n    M = hidden_states.shape[0]\n    E = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]\n    print(f\"INTROSPECT: M={M}, E={E}, config={config}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 1. List importable symbols from aiter.fused_moe\n    from aiter import fused_moe as fm_module\n    symbols = [s for s in dir(fm_module) if not s.startswith('_')]\n    print(f\"fused_moe symbols: {symbols}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 2. Check aiter top-level for sorting and stage kernels\n    import aiter\n    for name in ['moe_sorting_fwd', 'moe_sorting_opus_fwd', 'fmoe_g1u1',\n                 'ck_moe_2stages', 'cktile_moe_2stages']:\n        obj = getattr(aiter, name, None)\n        if obj is not None:\n            print(f\"  aiter.{name} EXISTS\", file=sys.stderr)\n        else:\n            print(f\"  aiter.{name} NOT FOUND\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 4. Print fused_moe_ function source (key dispatch logic) — first 120 lines\n    try:\n        src = inspect.getsource(fm_module.fused_moe_)\n        lines = src.split('\\n')[:120]\n        print(f\"\\n=== fused_moe_ source (first 120 lines) ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n        for line in lines:\n            print(line, file=sys.stderr)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Could not get fused_moe_ source: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 5. Check for 1-stage dict entries relevant to MXFP4\n    try:\n        from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe_1stage_dict\n        print(f\"\\n=== 1-stage dict keys ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n        for arch, entries in fused_moe_1stage_dict.items():\n            print(f\"  arch={arch}:\", file=sys.stderr)\n            for key, val in entries.items():\n                print(f\"    {key} -> {val}\", file=sys.stderr)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Could not read 1stage dict: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # Delegate to reference for correct output\n    from reference import ref_kernel\n    return ref_kernel(data)\n"
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Key findings from MoE introspection probe:**

1. **`ck_moe_stage2` is NOT in `fused_moe` module** — but the JIT build log reveals it as `ck_moe_stage2(inter_states, w1, w2, ...)` with a full signature. It's injected dynamically via the JIT `module_moe_ck2stages_*` build, accessed as `aiter.ck_moe_stage2` (not from `fused_moe`).

2. **`aiter.fmoe_g1u1` EXISTS** at the top level — this is the 1-stage fused kernel that's gated off by `run_1stage=False`. It maps to `(Silu, per_1x32, bf16, fp4x2, fp4x2, True, False)` in both gfx942 and gfx950 dicts. This is a potential speedup path!

3. **`fused_moe_` source was truncated** — `inspect.getsource` returned the JIT wrapper closure, not the actual `fused_moe_` dispatch logic. Need a deeper probe to get the real dispatch source.

4. **CK 2-stage JIT module** reveals the full HIP-level signatures for `ck_moe_stage1` and `ck_moe_stage2`, including the critical `is_shuffled=True` parameter and `kernelName` string selection.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"GEMM probe — dump aiter configs, gemm_afp4wfp4 API, and source paths.\"\"\"\nimport sys\nimport os\nimport inspect\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    import aiter\n\n    # 1. Print aiter path and version\n    aiter_path = os.path.dirname(aiter.__file__)\n    print(f\"aiter path: {aiter_path}\", file=sys.stderr)\n    print(f\"aiter version: {getattr(aiter, '__version__', 'unknown')}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 2. Walk configs directory — print ONLY filenames and sizes (not content)\n    configs_path = os.path.join(aiter_path, \"configs\")\n    if os.path.isdir(configs_path):\n        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(configs_path):\n            rel = os.path.relpath(root, aiter_path)\n            for f in sorted(files):\n                fpath = os.path.join(root, f)\n                sz = os.path.getsize(fpath)\n                print(f\"CONFIG: {rel}/{f} ({sz} bytes)\", file=sys.stderr)\n    model_configs = os.path.join(aiter_path, \"configs\", \"model_configs\")\n    if os.path.isdir(model_configs):\n        for f in sorted(os.listdir(model_configs)):\n            fpath = os.path.join(model_configs, f)\n            sz = os.path.getsize(fpath)\n            print(f\"MODEL_CONFIG: {f} ({sz} bytes)\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 3. Print dsv3_fp4 CSV content (this is the one for our competition shapes)\n    dsv3_path = os.path.join(aiter_path, \"configs\", \"model_configs\",\n                             \"dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv\")\n    if os.path.exists(dsv3_path):\n        print(f\"\\n=== dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n        with open(dsv3_path) as fh:\n            print(fh.read(), file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 4. Check gemm_afp4wfp4 import and signature\n    try:\n        from aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4 import gemm_afp4wfp4\n        sig = inspect.signature(gemm_afp4wfp4)\n        print(f\"\\ngemm_afp4wfp4 signature: {sig}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        src_lines = inspect.getsource(gemm_afp4wfp4).split('\\n')[:40]\n        print(f\"\\n=== gemm_afp4wfp4 source (first 40 lines) ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n        for line in src_lines:\n            print(line, file=sys.stderr)\n    except ImportError as e:\n        print(f\"gemm_afp4wfp4 NOT importable: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"gemm_afp4wfp4 error: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 5. Check fmoe_g1u1 signature (1-stage MoE kernel)\n    try:\n        g1u1 = getattr(aiter, 'fmoe_g1u1', None)\n        if g1u1:\n            try:\n                sig = inspect.signature(g1u1)\n                print(f\"\\naiter.fmoe_g1u1 signature: {sig}\", file=sys.stderr)\n            except (ValueError, TypeError):\n                print(f\"\\naiter.fmoe_g1u1 exists but no signature\", file=sys.stderr)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"fmoe_g1u1 check error: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 6. List gemm-related ops in aiter\n    gemm_ops = [s for s in dir(aiter) if 'gemm' in s.lower() or 'a4w4' in s.lower()]\n    print(f\"\\naiter gemm ops: {gemm_ops}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # 7. Check dynamic_mxfp4_quant source for optimization hints\n    try:\n        from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant\n        src_lines = inspect.getsource(dynamic_mxfp4_quant).split('\\n')[:30]\n        print(f\"\\n=== dynamic_mxfp4_quant source (first 30 lines) ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n        for line in src_lines:\n            print(line, file=sys.stderr)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"dynamic_mxfp4_quant source error: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # Delegate to reference for correctness\n    from reference import ref_kernel\n    return ref_kernel(data)\n"
```

   107→def gemm_afp4wfp4(
   108→    x: torch.Tensor,
   109→    w: torch.Tensor,
   110→    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
   111→    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
   112→    dtype: Optional[torch.dtype] = torch.bfloat16,

   132→=== dynamic_mxfp4_quant source (first 30 lines) ===
   133→def dynamic_mxfp4_quant(
   134→    x: torch.Tensor, scaling_mode: str = "even"
   135→) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
   136→    """
   137→    Quantize a tensor to MX FP4 format.
   138→
   139→    Args:
   140→        x: The input tensor, typically fp16 or bf16.
   141→        scaling_mode: The method to calculate MX block scaling.
   142→            - "even" (default): `even_round` in `quark.torch.quantization.utils`.
   143→            - etc.
   144→    Returns:
   145→        A tuple of (x_fp4, blockscale_e8m0).
   146→    """
   147→    _LOGGER.info(f"DYNAMIC_MXFP4_QUANT: x={tuple(x.shape)}")
   148→    # Assume x is 2D-Tensor for now
   149→    M, N = x.shape
   150→
   151→    assert (N // 2) % 2 == 0
   152→
   153→    # This is fixed by spec for MXFP4. Do not tune this.
   154→    MXFP4_QUANT_BLOCK_SIZE = 32
   155→    x_fp4 = torch.empty((M, N // 2), dtype=torch.uint8, device=x.device)
   156→    blockscale_e8m0 = torch.empty(
   157→        ((N + MXFP4_QUANT_BLOCK_SIZE - 1) // MXFP4_QUANT_BLOCK_SIZE, M),
   158→        dtype=torch.uint8,
   159→        device=x.device,
   160→    ).T
   161→
   162→    # for large N values

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Critical findings from GEMM probe:**

1. **`gemm_afp4wfp4` is importable and has a clean API:** `gemm_afp4wfp4(x, w, x_scales, w_scales, dtype=bf16, y=None, config=None, skip_reduce=False)`. It expects `x.shape = (M, K)` and `w.shape = (N, K)` — so tensors need to be in their quantized packed form (K/2 for fp4).

2. **`gemm_a4w4_asm` full signature revealed:** `gemm_a4w4_asm(A, B, A_scale, B_scale, out, kernelName, bias=None, alpha=1.0, beta=0.0, bpreshuffle=True, log2_k_split=None)`. Two HIP kernels exist: `32x128` (M≤32) and `192x128` (M>32).

3. **DSV3 FP4 tuned CSV** shows kernel configs for 257-expert shapes. Key: at `token=64`, stage1 switches from `64x32x32x128` tile to `256x32x128x128_1x4` — indicating larger tiles unlock more parallelism. At token=16384, stage2 switches to `flydsl_moe2_afp4_wfp4_bf16_t64x256x256_reduce` (a FlyDSL kernel!).

4. **`aiter.fmoe_g1u1` has signature `(*args, **kwargs)`** — it's a JIT-wrapped function. We'd need to introspect its torch.ops schema to understand the real parameters.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**GEMM strategy with `gemm_afp4wfp4`:**

The key insight from the probe is that `gemm_afp4wfp4` takes `x.shape = (M, K)` and `w.shape = (N, K)` — these are the packed fp4 shapes (K/2 bytes). Per the `aiter-mxfp4-api-limitations` skill (Limitation 1), we need `.view(torch.uint8)` on all tensors to avoid the `KeyError: 'REDACTED'`. But the skill also warns that even with uint8 views, results may be wrong due to different byte interpretation.

However, we now have the **actual source** — it calls `_get_config(M, N, K)` for auto-tuning, then dispatches to `gemm_afp4wfp4_` which is a Triton JIT kernel. If it works, the Triton auto-tuner might find a faster config than the two fixed ASM tiles (`32x128` and `192x128`).

**Plan:** Try `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` for A (which we know works) + pass B_q directly (not shuffled — `gemm_afp4wfp4` likely doesn't need shuffle since it's Triton, not CK).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"GEMM — Try gemm_afp4wfp4 Triton kernel with auto-tuning and split-K.\n\ngemm_afp4wfp4 API (from probe):\n  gemm_afp4wfp4(x, w, x_scales, w_scales, dtype=bf16, y=None, config=None, skip_reduce=False)\n  x: (M, K/2) uint8 — packed fp4 activations\n  w: (N, K/2) uint8 — packed fp4 weights\n  x_scales: E8M0 scales as uint8\n  w_scales: E8M0 scales as uint8\n\nKey: all tensors must be .view(torch.uint8) to avoid KeyError on native fp4 dtype.\nUses dynamic_mxfp4_quant for A (known working), B_q from input (un-shuffled).\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import dtypes\nfrom aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant\nfrom aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4 import gemm_afp4wfp4\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n\n    # Quantize A using the patched Triton kernel\n    A_fp4, A_scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)\n\n    # B_q is already quantized (from reference's get_triton_quant with shuffle=True)\n    # But gemm_afp4wfp4 may not want shuffled data — try B_q (un-shuffled quant output)\n    # B_q shape: (N, K/2) as fp4x2 dtype\n    # B_scale_sh is shuffled — we need un-shuffled B_scale for gemm_afp4wfp4\n\n    # Re-quantize B to get un-shuffled scale (B_q data is same either way)\n    # Actually, B_q from input IS the un-shuffled quant data (before shuffle_weight)\n    # B_scale_sh is the shuffled scale — we need to unshuffle it\n    # Use e8m0_unshuffle to recover un-shuffled B_scale\n    from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle\n\n    # e8m0_unshuffle: inverse permute of (0,3,5,2,4,1) is (0,5,3,1,4,2)\n    n = B.shape[0]\n    k = B.shape[1]\n    B_scale_sh_u8 = B_scale_sh.view(torch.uint8)\n    sm, sn = B_scale_sh_u8.shape\n    # Inverse of e8m0_shuffle\n    scale = B_scale_sh_u8.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)\n    scale = scale.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()\n    B_scale = scale.view(sm, sn)[:n, :k // 32]\n\n    # All tensors as uint8 for gemm_afp4wfp4 (avoids KeyError on native fp4 dtype)\n    return gemm_afp4wfp4(\n        A_fp4.view(torch.uint8),\n        B_q.view(torch.uint8),\n        A_scale_e8m0.view(torch.uint8),\n        B_scale.contiguous().view(torch.uint8),\n        dtype=dtypes.bf16,\n    )\n"
}
```

Interesting results! Let me compare the `gemm_afp4wfp4` Triton results against the current `dynamic_mxfp4_quant + gemm_a4w4` baseline.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**gemm_afp4wfp4 Triton vs current gemm_a4w4 ASM benchmark comparison:**

| Shape (m x n x k) | gemm_afp4wfp4 (Triton) | gemm_a4w4 (ASM, baseline ~24 us geomean) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 x 2880 x 512 | 17.2 µs | ~8 µs (est) | Triton needs auto-tune warmup |
| 16 x 2112 x 7168 | 30.1 µs | ~34 µs (est) | Triton faster on large K! |
| 32 x 4096 x 512 | 16.3 µs | ~15 µs (est) | Similar |
| 32 x 2880 x 512 | 16.4 µs | ~15 µs (est) | Similar |
| 64 x 7168 x 2048 | 21.2 µs | ~24 µs (est) | Triton faster! |
| 256 x 3072 x 1536 | 21.8 µs | ~31 µs (est) | Triton much faster! |

The Triton kernel's geomean is ~20 µs, potentially better than the current ~24 µs! But I need to also account for the `e8m0_unshuffle` overhead. The real comparison requires benchmarking the ASM baseline too. Let me restore the ASM version and benchmark it.

**Key observation:** The `gemm_afp4wfp4` kernel uses an auto-tuner (`_get_config`) to select tile configurations per shape, while the ASM kernel only has two fixed tiles. For larger M (64, 256), the auto-tuner can select better-fitting tiles.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**MoE 1-stage kernel strategy:**

The `fmoe_g1u1` kernel is a fused 1-stage MoE that combines gate-up GEMM, SiLU activation, and down GEMM in a single kernel launch. For the 2-stage path, there's overhead from:
1. Stage 1 kernel launch + intermediate buffer allocation
2. Activation computation (SiLU)
3. Stage 2 kernel launch

A 1-stage kernel eliminates the intermediate buffer and one kernel launch, potentially saving 10-30 µs on small shapes. The key question is whether `fmoe_g1u1` correctly handles MXFP4 (`per_1x32`) quantized weights.

From the introspection, the 1-stage dict entry for gfx950 is:
`(Silu, per_1x32, bf16, fp4x2, fp4x2, True, False)` → exists!

The `True` flag is `use_g1u1` and `False` is `doweight_stage1`. This matches our use case exactly.

However, `fmoe_g1u1` has `(*args, **kwargs)` signature — it's a JIT wrapper. We need to figure out the actual parameters by looking at how `fused_moe_1stage` calls it.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me write an introspection probe for `fmoe_g1u1` to understand its API, combined with trying to actually call it.

Excellent! Now I have the full picture. Let me read the rest of the output for the `fused_moe_` source.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Critical discovery: `fused_moe_` is a torch.ops.aiter registered op!**

The `fused_moe_` function we see in Python is actually a JIT wrapper around `torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_`. Its schema reveals:
```
aiter::fused_moe_(hidden_states, w1, w2, topk_weight, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
  activation=0, quant_type=0, doweight_stage1=False, w1_scale=None, w2_scale=None,
  a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None, block_size_M=-1, num_local_tokens=None,
  moe_sorting_dispatch_policy=0, dtype=None, hidden_pad=0, intermediate_pad=0,
  bias1=None, bias2=None) -> Tensor
```

This is a **single C++ op** that handles sorting + 2-stage dispatch internally! The Python `fused_moe()` function just calls this.

For `fmoe_g1u1` — the `else` branch in `fused_moe_1stage` shows that for `per_1x32` quant it goes through `get_quant(quant_type)` to quantize activations first, then calls `fmoe_g1u1`. But this quantization uses `get_hip_quant` (the internal MoE quant backend), which may or may not produce correct output for the 1-stage kernel.

**Key insight: We can't easily bypass the 2-stage dispatch.** The `fused_moe_` torch op handles everything internally (sorting, kernel selection, 2-stage execution). The only tuning knobs exposed are `block_size_M`, env vars like `AITER_KSPLIT`, and the `moe_sorting_dispatch_policy` parameter.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The `fused_moe_` is a monolithic torch op — calling individual stages from Python would require replicating its internal sorting logic. The 1-stage `fmoe_g1u1` path goes through `fused_moe_1stage` which needs pre-sorted data. Let me try calling `fused_moe_1stage` directly with the correct parameters.

But first, let me assess: is the 1-stage path even promising? The `fused_moe_1stage` source shows for `per_1x32` quant, it goes through the `else` branch which quantizes activations with `get_quant(quant_type)` (which is `get_hip_quant`). This is the same quant that works for 2-stage, so it should be fine. But the 1-stage kernel `fmoe_g1u1` needs to be actually called — the source was truncated.

Let me try a simpler approach first: **calling `torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_` directly** with different parameters to see if we can unlock better performance. This avoids the Python overhead of the `fused_moe()` wrapper.

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"MoE — Try calling torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_ directly with MXFP4 params.\n\nBypass the Python fused_moe() wrapper which has overhead from:\n- get_2stage_cfgs() CSV lookup\n- ksplit heuristic computation\n- Environment variable parsing\n- Python dispatch logic\n\nThe torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_ registered op handles sorting + 2-stage internally.\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType, dtypes\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    (\n        hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights, topk_ids, config,\n    ) = data\n\n    num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]\n    estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts\n\n    # Set env vars for KSPLIT selection (read by the C++ op at dispatch time)\n    if estimated_m >= 50:\n        os.environ.pop(\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\", None)\n        os.environ.pop(\"AITER_KSPLIT\", None)\n    elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:\n        os.environ[\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\"] = \"1\"\n        os.environ[\"AITER_KSPLIT\"] = \"4\"\n    else:\n        os.environ[\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\"] = \"1\"\n        os.environ[\"AITER_KSPLIT\"] = \"2\"\n\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    # Call torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_ directly — this is the C++ registered op\n    # that handles sorting + 2-stage dispatch internally.\n    # The Python fused_moe() wrapper adds overhead we don't need.\n    return torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_(\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        None,  # expert_mask\n        ActivationType.Silu.value,  # activation (SymInt)\n        QuantType.per_1x32.value,   # quant_type (SymInt)\n        False,  # doweight_stage1\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,  # w1_scale\n        down_weight_scale_shuffled,     # w2_scale\n        None,  # a1_scale\n        None,  # a2_scale\n        -1,    # block_size_M (auto)\n        None,  # num_local_tokens\n        False, # moe_sorting_dispatch_policy\n        dtypes.bf16,  # dtype\n        hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad,\n        None,  # bias1\n        None,  # bias2\n    )\n"
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**MoE result: calling `torch.ops.aiter.fused_moe_` directly produces identical performance to the Python wrapper.** The 155.6 µs geomean is within noise of the 155.5 µs Phase 10 baseline. This makes sense — the Python wrapper overhead is trivially small compared to the 60-354 µs kernel execution times. The real dispatch happens in C++.

**Conclusion:** There's no Python-level overhead to eliminate in the MoE path. The 1.07x gap to the leader (145 µs) must come from kernel-level optimization, not dispatch optimization. The remaining approaches would be:
1. **1-stage `fmoe_g1u1`** — but this requires understanding its internal quantization flow
2. **Custom CK kernel names** (from the CSV dump) — but the kernelName='' means the C++ op selects its own
3. **The gap is only 7%** — likely the leader has access to better-tuned CK configs or a custom Triton kernel
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The direct torch.ops approach is equivalent. Let me restore the cleaner Python wrapper version (which is more readable) and mark this task complete, then submit the MoE to leaderboard with the original Phase 10 code which has identical performance.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMXFP4 MoE — Phase 10: Expert-count-aware KSPLIT selection.\n\nKey discovery: Optimal KSPLIT depends on BOTH estimated_m AND num_experts.\nFor large expert counts (257E, dexp=256), KSPLIT=4 is better at estimated_m<10\nbecause smaller expert weight matrices benefit more from K-dimension splitting.\nFor small expert counts (33E, dexp=512+), KSPLIT=2 remains optimal for sparse.\n\nStrategy:\n  - num_experts >= 200 AND estimated_m < 10: KSPLIT=4 (large expert count, sparse)\n  - estimated_m < 50 (others): KSPLIT=2 (moderate sparse, or small expert count)\n  - estimated_m >= 50: KSPLIT=0 (dense, CK MXFP4 path)\n  Always BYPASS_TUNE for sparse shapes to override CSV-locked 257E configs.\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    (\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight,\n        down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale,\n        down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        config,\n    ) = data\n\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]\n    estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts\n\n    if estimated_m >= 50:\n        # Dense: CK MXFP4-optimized path (ksplit=0).\n        os.environ.pop(\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\", None)\n        os.environ.pop(\"AITER_KSPLIT\", None)\n    elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:\n        # Large expert count, very sparse: split_k=4 exploits K-parallelism.\n        # Smaller expert weights (dexp~256) benefit more from higher splits.\n        os.environ[\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\"] = \"1\"\n        os.environ[\"AITER_KSPLIT\"] = \"4\"\n    else:\n        # Sparse (few experts or moderate token density): cktile split_k=2.\n        os.environ[\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\"] = \"1\"\n        os.environ[\"AITER_KSPLIT\"] = \"2\"\n\n    return fused_moe(\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        expert_mask=None,\n        activation=ActivationType.Silu,\n        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,\n        doweight_stage1=False,\n        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        a1_scale=None,\n        a2_scale=None,\n        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,\n    )\n"
}
```

```
    30→**Goal:** Optimize all 3 kernels to improve leaderboard rankings. Priority: MoE (quick win) -> MLA (highest impact potential) -> GEMM (blocked by JIT bug).
    31→
    32→**Architecture:** Each kernel is a single submission.py that exports custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t. Evaluated remotely on MI355X via Popcorn CLI (~2 min/submit). Scoring = geometric mean of benchmark runtimes. No local testing possible (gfx1151 not supported by ROCm 6.2.4).
    33→
    34→**Tech Stack:** Python, PyTorch, Triton (AMD ROCm), aiter library (AMD optimized kernels), Popcorn CLI.
    35→
    36→## Scope
    37→
    38→### In Scope
    39→
    40→- Optimize MoE kernel parameters (fused_moe tuning, stage config, sorting)
    41→- Optimize MLA kernel (NUM_KV_SPLITS tuning, batch-size dispatch, MXFP4 KV)
    42→- Fix GEMM JIT call-site issue to enable optimization
    43→- Research competitor approaches via aiter source and GPU MODE community
    44→- Leaderboard submissions for improved kernels
    45→
    46→### Out of Scope
    47→
    48→- Custom HIP C++ / assembly kernels (Python + aiter only for now)
    49→- Local testing (all via Popcorn CLI remote submission)
    50→- Changes to reference.py, task.py, eval.py, utils.py (competition files)
    51→- Phase 2 hackathon work (end-to-end DeepSeek-R1)
    52→
    53→## Context for Implementer
    54→
    55→> **This is iterative GPU kernel optimization. Each change requires a remote submission (~2 min).**
    56→
    57→**Workflow per optimization attempt:**
    58→1. Modify submission.py
    59→2. popcorn-cli submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard <name> <file> (correctness)
    60→3. If passes: --mode benchmark (timing without leaderboard impact)
    61→4. If faster: --mode leaderboard (official ranked submission)
    62→
    63→**Key constraints:**
    64→- Benchmark clears L2 cache between runs -- no cache warming tricks
    65→- GEMM benchmark includes A quantization time (part of timed region)
    66→- Correctness tolerances: GEMM rtol=1e-2, MLA rtol=1e-2, MoE rtol=5e-2
    67→- Transient artifact download failures happen -- retry on unexplained failure
    68→- No per-user rate limit; submit freely
    69→
    70→### Key Files
    71→
    72→| File | Purpose |
    73→|------|---------|
    74→| kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py | MoE submission (59 lines, calls fused_moe) |
    75→| kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py | MLA submission (143 lines, mla_decode_fwd persistent mode) |
    76→| kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py | GEMM submission (22 lines, delegates to ref_kernel) |
    77→| kernels/*/reference.py | Reference implementations (read-only, study for optimization hints) |
    78→| results.md | Competition results documentation |
    79→
    80→### Benchmark Configurations
    81→
    82→**MoE** (7 configs, geometric mean):
    83→- TP=8: bs={16,128,512}, dhidden=7168, dexpert=256, E=257, topk=9
    84→- TP=4: bs={16,128,512}, dhidden=7168, dexpert=512, E=33, topk=9
    85→- EP:   bs=512, dhidden=7168, dexpert=2048, E=33, topk=9
    86→
    87→**MLA** (8 configs, geometric mean):
    88→- bs={4,32,64,256} x kvseqlen={1024,8192}, qseqlen=1, tp=4 or 8
    89→
```

```
     1→"""GEMM — Try gemm_afp4wfp4 Triton kernel with auto-tuning and split-K.
     2→
     3→gemm_afp4wfp4 API (from probe):
     4→  gemm_afp4wfp4(x, w, x_scales, w_scales, dtype=bf16, y=None, config=None, skip_reduce=False)
     5→  x: (M, K/2) uint8 — packed fp4 activations
     6→  w: (N, K/2) uint8 — packed fp4 weights
     7→  x_scales: E8M0 scales as uint8
     8→  w_scales: E8M0 scales as uint8
     9→
    10→Key: all tensors must be .view(torch.uint8) to avoid KeyError on native fp4 dtype.
    11→Uses dynamic_mxfp4_quant for A (known working), B_q from input (un-shuffled).
    12→"""
    13→import torch
    14→from task import input_t, output_t
    15→from aiter import dtypes
    16→from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    17→from aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4 import gemm_afp4wfp4
    18→
    19→
    20→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    21→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    22→
    23→    # Quantize A using the patched Triton kernel
    24→    A_fp4, A_scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)
    25→
    26→    # B_q is already quantized (from reference's get_triton_quant with shuffle=True)
    27→    # But gemm_afp4wfp4 may not want shuffled data — try B_q (un-shuffled quant output)
    28→    # B_q shape: (N, K/2) as fp4x2 dtype
    29→    # B_scale_sh is shuffled — we need un-shuffled B_scale for gemm_afp4wfp4
    30→
    31→    # Re-quantize B to get un-shuffled scale (B_q data is same either way)
    32→    # Actually, B_q from input IS the un-shuffled quant data (before shuffle_weight)
    33→    # B_scale_sh is the shuffled scale — we need to unshuffle it
    34→    # Use e8m0_unshuffle to recover un-shuffled B_scale
    35→    from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
    36→
    37→    # e8m0_unshuffle: inverse permute of (0,3,5,2,4,1) is (0,5,3,1,4,2)
    38→    n = B.shape[0]
    39→    k = B.shape[1]
    40→    B_scale_sh_u8 = B_scale_sh.view(torch.uint8)
    41→    sm, sn = B_scale_sh_u8.shape
    42→    # Inverse of e8m0_shuffle
    43→    scale = B_scale_sh_u8.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
    44→    scale = scale.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()
    45→    B_scale = scale.view(sm, sn)[:n, :k // 32]
    46→
    47→    # All tensors as uint8 for gemm_afp4wfp4 (avoids KeyError on native fp4 dtype)
    48→    return gemm_afp4wfp4(
    49→        A_fp4.view(torch.uint8),
    50→        B_q.view(torch.uint8),
    51→        A_scale_e8m0.view(torch.uint8),
    52→        B_scale.contiguous().view(torch.uint8),
    53→        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
    54→    )
    55→
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"GEMM — gemm_afp4wfp4 Triton kernel with auto-tuning (20.6 µs geomean, 2.12x leader gap).\n\nUses dynamic_mxfp4_quant for A quantization + e8m0_unshuffle for B_scale recovery.\nAll tensors viewed as uint8 to avoid KeyError on native fp4 dtype in gemm_afp4wfp4.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import dtypes\nfrom aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant\nfrom aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4 import gemm_afp4wfp4\n\n\ndef _e8m0_unshuffle(scale_sh: torch.Tensor, orig_m: int, orig_n: int) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Inverse of aiter's e8m0_shuffle: recover un-shuffled E8M0 scale from shuffled.\"\"\"\n    u8 = scale_sh.view(torch.uint8)\n    sm, sn = u8.shape\n    s = u8.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)\n    s = s.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()\n    return s.view(sm, sn)[:orig_m, :orig_n]\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n\n    A_fp4, A_scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)\n\n    n, k = B.shape\n    B_scale = _e8m0_unshuffle(B_scale_sh, orig_m=n, orig_n=k // 32)\n\n    return gemm_afp4wfp4(\n        A_fp4.view(torch.uint8),\n        B_q.view(torch.uint8),\n        A_scale_e8m0.view(torch.uint8),\n        B_scale.contiguous().view(torch.uint8),\n        dtype=dtypes.bf16,\n    )\n"
}
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Current standings & what the skills reveal:**
- **GEMM** (20.6µs, leader 9.7µs, 2.12x gap): The `tritonblas-matmul-fp4-api` skill reveals that **A-quantization is the pipeline bottleneck** (33-39µs in isolation). A **fused quant+GEMM kernel** that quantizes bf16→fp4 inside the GEMM loop would eliminate this overhead entirely.
- **tritonblas.matmul_fp4** exists on the runner with Origami chiplet-aware scheduling, but standalone it's ~26µs (worse than our gemm_afp4wfp4 at 20.6µs). The value is in its **Triton kernel source** — it shows the correct `tl.dot_scaled` patterns and Origami grid scheduling.
- **Helion** can generate correct `tl.dot_scaled("e2m1", ...)` Triton code locally on CPU — no GPU needed for code generation.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The strategy is clear: **use Helion locally to generate a fused quant+GEMM Triton kernel, borrowing Origami scheduling from tritonblas's fp4_matmul source.** Let me start by checking our local setup.

```
     5→import sys

    11→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:

    46→        print(f"B_scale_shuffled shape: {B_scale_shuffled.shape}, dtype: {B_scale_shuffled.dtype}",
    47→              file=sys.stderr)
    48→
    49→        # Try to find the permutation pattern by checking first row
    50→        if not shuffle_match and n > 0:
    51→            row0_truth = B_scale_truth_u8[0]
    52→            row0_sh = B_scale_sh_u8[0]
    53→            print(f"First row truth (first 16): {row0_truth[:16].tolist()}", file=sys.stderr)
    54→            print(f"First row shuffled (first 16): {row0_sh[:16].tolist()}", file=sys.stderr)
    55→
    56→        # If B_q matches, try using B_q + B_scale_truth (skip B re-quant in future)
    57→        if B_q_match:
    58→            print("OPTIMIZATION: Can use input B_q directly!", file=sys.stderr)
    59→
    60→    except Exception as e:
    61→        print(f"PROBE ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
    62→        import traceback
    63→        traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)
    64→
    65→    return ref_kernel(data)
    66→
```

```
     1→"""
     2→MXFP4 GEMM via Triton tl.dot_scaled — bypasses aiter's ASM kernel entirely.
     3→Helion-generated kernel structure, adapted for competition submission format.
     4→
     5→Scale layout learned from runner: for data tile [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K],
     6→scale must be [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K // 16] (one scale per 32 fp4 = 16 packed bytes).
     7→"""
     8→import sys
     9→import torch
    10→import triton
    11→import triton.language as tl
    12→from task import input_t, output_t
    13→from reference import ref_kernel
    14→
    15→
    16→@triton.jit
    17→def _mxfp4_gemm_kernel(
    18→    A_ptr, A_scale_ptr,
    19→    B_ptr, B_scale_ptr,
    20→    C_ptr,
    21→    M, N, K_HALF,
    22→    stride_a_m, stride_a_k,
    23→    stride_as_m, stride_as_k,
    24→    stride_b_k, stride_b_n,
    25→    stride_bs_n, stride_bs_k,
    26→    stride_c_m, stride_c_n,
    27→    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr,
    28→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,
    29→    BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,
    30→):
    31→    """MXFP4 x MXFP4 GEMM using tl.dot_scaled.
    32→
    33→    A_q:     [M, K//2] packed fp4 (uint8)
    34→    A_scale: [M, K//32] e8m0 scales
    35→    B_q_t:   [K//2, N] packed fp4 (uint8) — B transposed
    36→    B_scale_t: [K//32, N] e8m0 scales — B scales transposed
    37→    C:       [M, N] bf16 output
    38→    """
    39→    pid_m = tl.program_id(0)
    40→    pid_n = tl.program_id(1)
    41→
    42→    offs_m = pid_m * BLOCK_M + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_M)
    43→    offs_n = pid_n * BLOCK_N + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
    44→
    45→    mask_m = offs_m < M
    46→    mask_n = offs_n < N
    47→
    48→    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N], dtype=tl.float32)
    49→
    50→    # Scale covers 32 fp4 elements = 16 packed bytes per scale entry
    51→    # So for BLOCK_K packed bytes, we need BLOCK_K // 16 scale entries
    52→    SCALE_PER_BLOCK: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // 16
    53→
    54→    for k_start in range(0, K_HALF, BLOCK_K):
    55→        offs_k = k_start + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)
    56→        mask_k = offs_k < K_HALF
    57→
    58→        # A tile [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K] — packed fp4 data
    59→        a = tl.load(
    60→            A_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_a_m + offs_k[None, :] * stride_a_k,
    61→            mask=mask_m[:, None] & mask_k[None, :],
    62→            other=0,
    63→        )
    64→
    65→        # A scale for this K-tile: [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]
    66→        # Scale index = k_start // 16 (16 packed bytes per scale group)
    67→        scale_k_start = k_start // 16
    68→        offs_scale_k = scale_k_start + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_BLOCK)
    69→        a_scale = tl.load(
    70→            A_scale_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_as_m + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_as_k,
    71→            mask=mask_m[:, None],
    72→            other=0,
    73→        )
    74→
    75→        # B tile [BLOCK_K, BLOCK_N] — B already transposed to [K//2, N]
    76→        b = tl.load(
    77→            B_ptr + offs_k[:, None] * stride_b_k + offs_n[None, :] * stride_b_n,
    78→            mask=mask_k[:, None] & mask_n[None, :],
    79→            other=0,
    80→        )
    81→
    82→        # B scale for this K-tile: [BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]
    83→        # rhs_scale keeps N dimension first (matches B's original [N, K//32] layout)
    84→        b_scale = tl.load(
    85→            B_scale_ptr + offs_n[:, None] * stride_bs_n + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_bs_k,
    86→            mask=mask_n[:, None],
    87→            other=0,
    88→        )
    89→
    90→        acc = tl.dot_scaled(a, a_scale, "e2m1", b, b_scale, "e2m1", acc=acc)
    91→
    92→    c = acc.to(tl.bfloat16)
    93→    tl.store(
    94→        C_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_c_m + offs_n[None, :] * stride_c_n,
    95→        c,
    96→        mask=mask_m[:, None] & mask_n[None, :],
    97→    )
    98→
    99→
   100→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   101→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
   102→
   103→    try:
   104→        from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
   105→
   106→        m, k = A.shape
   107→        n = B.shape[0]
   108→        k_half = k // 2
   109→
   110→        # Quantize A (un-shuffled, raw fp4 + e8m0 scale)
   111→        A_fp4, A_scale = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())
   112→        A_scale = A_scale.view(torch.uint8)
   113→
   114→        # B_q from input matches dynamic_mxfp4_quant output (verified).
   115→        # Re-quant B to get un-shuffled scale (data is discarded).
   116→        _, B_scale = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(B.contiguous())
   117→        B_scale = B_scale.view(torch.uint8)
   118→
   119→        # Use input B_q directly (skip re-quant data copy)
   120→        B_fp4_t = B_q.view(torch.uint8).t().contiguous()
   121→
   122→        # Block sizes — must be power of 2
   123→        # Minimum BLOCK_M=16 — tl.dot_scaled on gfx950 may require it
   124→        BLOCK_M = max(16, min(64, triton.next_power_of_2(m)))
   125→        BLOCK_N = min(64, triton.next_power_of_2(n))
   126→        # BLOCK_K in packed fp4 space. Must be >= 64 for AMD gfx950 dot_scaled
   127→        BLOCK_K = 64
   128→
   129→        C = torch.empty(m, n, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=A.device)
   130→
   131→        grid = (triton.cdiv(m, BLOCK_M), triton.cdiv(n, BLOCK_N))
   132→
   133→        _mxfp4_gemm_kernel[grid](
   134→            A_fp4, A_scale,
   135→            B_fp4_t, B_scale,
   136→            C,
   137→            m, n, k_half,
   138→            A_fp4.stride(0), A_fp4.stride(1),
   139→            A_scale.stride(0), A_scale.stride(1),
   140→            B_fp4_t.stride(0), B_fp4_t.stride(1),
   141→            B_scale.stride(0), B_scale.stride(1),
   142→            C.stride(0), C.stride(1),
   143→            BLOCK_M=BLOCK_M,
   144→            BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N,
   145→            BLOCK_K=BLOCK_K,
   146→        )
   147→        return C
   148→
   149→    except Exception as e:
   150→        print(f"TRITON DOT_SCALED ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   151→        import traceback
   152→        traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)
   153→        return ref_kernel(data)
   154→
```

```
     1→"""Fused bf16→fp4 quant + GEMM using tl.dot_scaled + Origami scheduling.
     2→
     3→Takes bf16 A directly, quantizes on-the-fly inside the GEMM kernel.
     4→Eliminates the separate quantization kernel launch overhead (~33 µs).
     5→Uses Origami chiplet-aware scheduling from tritonblas.
     6→"""
     7→import sys
     8→import time
     9→import torch
    10→import triton
    11→import triton.language as tl
    12→from task import input_t, output_t
    13→
    14→
    15→def e8m0_unshuffle(scale_shuffled, orig_m, orig_n):
    16→    sm, sn = scale_shuffled.shape
    17→    scale = scale_shuffled.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
    18→    scale = scale.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()
    19→    scale = scale.view(sm, sn)
    20→    return scale[:orig_m, :orig_n]
    21→
    22→
    23→@triton.jit
    24→def _fused_quant_gemm_kernel(
    25→    # A is bf16 [M, K] — quantized on-the-fly
    26→    A_ptr,
    27→    # B is fp4 packed [K//2, N] (transposed by caller)
    28→    B_ptr,
    29→    # Output C is bf16 [M, N]
    30→    C_ptr,
    31→    # B scale [N, K//32] uint8
    32→    B_scale_ptr,
    33→    # Dimensions
    34→    M, N, K,  # K is in bf16 elements (NOT packed bytes)
    35→    # A strides (bf16)
    36→    stride_am, stride_ak,
    37→    # B strides (transposed: [K//2, N])
    38→    stride_bk, stride_bn,
    39→    # B_scale strides [N, K//32]
    40→    stride_bs_n, stride_bs_k,
    41→    # C strides
    42→    stride_cm, stride_cn,
    43→    # Tile sizes
    44→    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr,
    45→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,
    46→    BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,  # in bf16 elements (will be K//2 packed bytes for fp4)
    47→    # Origami scheduling
    48→    GROUP_SIZE_M: tl.constexpr,
    49→    NUM_XCDS: tl.constexpr,
    50→    CHUNK_SIZE: tl.constexpr,
    51→    # FP4 constants
    52→    SCALE_GROUP_SIZE: tl.constexpr,  # 32 bf16 elements per scale
    53→):
    54→    # Origami chiplet-aware PID transform
    55→    pid = tl.program_id(0)
    56→    total_blocks_M = tl.cdiv(M, BLOCK_M)
    57→    total_blocks_N = tl.cdiv(N, BLOCK_N)
    58→
    59→    # Chiplet-chunked scheduling (from tritonblas fp4_matmul)
    60→    num_chunks = tl.cdiv(total_blocks_M * total_blocks_N, CHUNK_SIZE)
    61→    chunk_id = pid // CHUNK_SIZE
    62→    chunk_lane = pid % CHUNK_SIZE
    63→
    64→    # Remap chunk to XCD
    65→    xcd_id = chunk_id % NUM_XCDS
    66→    chunk_in_xcd = chunk_id // NUM_XCDS
    67→    remapped_chunk = xcd_id * tl.cdiv(num_chunks, NUM_XCDS) + chunk_in_xcd
    68→    remapped_pid = remapped_chunk * CHUNK_SIZE + chunk_lane
    69→
    70→    if remapped_pid >= total_blocks_M * total_blocks_N:
    71→        return
    72→
    73→    # Group-M tile ordering
    74→    num_pid_in_group = GROUP_SIZE_M * total_blocks_N
    75→    group_id = remapped_pid // num_pid_in_group
    76→    first_pid_m = group_id * GROUP_SIZE_M
    77→    group_size_m = min(total_blocks_M - first_pid_m, GROUP_SIZE_M)
    78→    pid_m = first_pid_m + (remapped_pid % num_pid_in_group) % group_size_m
    79→    pid_n = (remapped_pid % num_pid_in_group) // group_size_m
    80→
    81→    offs_m = pid_m * BLOCK_M + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_M)
    82→    offs_n = pid_n * BLOCK_N + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
    83→    mask_m = offs_m < M
    84→    mask_n = offs_n < N
    85→
    86→    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N], dtype=tl.float32)
    87→
    88→    # BLOCK_K is in bf16 elements
    89→    # FP4 packs 2 elements per byte, so BLOCK_K_PACKED = BLOCK_K // 2
    90→    BLOCK_K_PACKED: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // 2
    91→    SCALE_PER_BLOCK: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    92→
    93→    K_PACKED = K // 2  # Total packed K dimension
    94→
    95→    for k_start in range(0, K, BLOCK_K):
    96→        offs_k = k_start + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)
    97→        mask_k = offs_k < K
    98→
    99→        # --- Load bf16 A tile [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K] ---
   100→        a_bf16 = tl.load(
   101→            A_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_am + offs_k[None, :] * stride_ak,
   102→            mask=mask_m[:, None] & mask_k[None, :],
   103→            other=0.0,
   104→        )
   105→
   106→        # --- Compute A scale: E8M0 per SCALE_GROUP_SIZE elements ---
   107→        # Reshape to [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK, SCALE_GROUP_SIZE]
   108→        a_reshaped = tl.reshape(a_bf16, [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK, SCALE_GROUP_SIZE])
   109→        # amax per group
   110→        a_abs = tl.abs(a_reshaped).to(tl.float32)
   111→        a_amax = tl.max(a_abs, axis=2)  # [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]
   112→        # E8M0 for fp4 e2m1: scale must satisfy amax / scale <= fp4_max (6.0)
   113→        # e = floor(log2(amax / 6.0)) + 128, clamped to [0, 254]
   114→        # This ensures normalized values stay within [-6, 6] (fp4 e2m1 range)
   115→        a_log2 = tl.where(
   116→            a_amax > 0,
   117→            tl.math.floor(tl.math.log2(a_amax / 6.0)) + 128.0,
   118→            0.0,
   119→        )
   120→        a_scale = tl.minimum(tl.maximum(a_log2, 0.0), 254.0).to(tl.uint8)
   121→
   122→        # --- Quantize A to fp4 e2m1 ---
   123→        a_scale_expanded = tl.reshape(
   124→            tl.broadcast_to(a_scale[:, :, None], [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK, SCALE_GROUP_SIZE]),
   125→            [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K]
   126→        ).to(tl.float32)
   127→        scale_factor = tl.math.exp2(a_scale_expanded - 127.0)
   128→        # Normalize by scale; post-norm values are in [-6, 6] for e2m1
   129→        a_normalized = tl.where(scale_factor > 0, a_bf16.to(tl.float32) / scale_factor, 0.0)
   130→
   131→        # Encode fp4 e2m1: 1 sign bit | 3 magnitude bits
   132→        # Values: 0, ±0.5, ±1, ±1.5, ±2, ±3, ±4, ±6
   133→        sign = (a_normalized < 0).to(tl.int32)
   134→        x_abs = tl.abs(a_normalized)
   135→        mag = tl.where(x_abs < 0.25, 0,
   136→              tl.where(x_abs < 0.75, 1,
   137→              tl.where(x_abs < 1.25, 2,
   138→              tl.where(x_abs < 1.75, 3,
   139→              tl.where(x_abs < 2.5,  4,
   140→              tl.where(x_abs < 3.5,  5,
   141→              tl.where(x_abs < 5.0,  6, 7)))))))
   142→        fp4_codes = (sign << 3) | mag  # [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K], values 0-15
   143→
   144→        # Pack adjacent pairs: shift odd-indexed elements to high nibble, then
   145→        # sum adjacent pairs. Since low/high nibble bits never overlap, sum = OR.
   146→        shift = tl.where(tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K) % 2 == 1, 4, 0)  # [BLOCK_K]
   147→        shifted = (fp4_codes & 0xF) << shift[None, :]  # [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K]
   148→        a_fp4 = tl.sum(
   149→            tl.reshape(shifted, [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K_PACKED, 2]), axis=2
   150→        ).to(tl.uint8)  # [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K_PACKED]
   151→
   152→        # --- Load B tile [BLOCK_K_PACKED, BLOCK_N] (already fp4 packed) ---
   153→        k_packed_start = k_start // 2
   154→        offs_k_packed = k_packed_start + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K_PACKED)
   155→        mask_k_packed = offs_k_packed < K_PACKED
   156→
   157→        b = tl.load(
   158→            B_ptr + offs_k_packed[:, None] * stride_bk + offs_n[None, :] * stride_bn,
   159→            mask=mask_k_packed[:, None] & mask_n[None, :],
   160→            other=0,
   161→        )
   162→
   163→        # --- Load B scale [BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_BLOCK] ---
   164→        scale_k_start = k_start // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
   165→        offs_scale_k = scale_k_start + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_BLOCK)
   166→        b_scale = tl.load(
   167→            B_scale_ptr + offs_n[:, None] * stride_bs_n + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_bs_k,
   168→            mask=mask_n[:, None],
   169→            other=0,
   170→        )
   171→
   172→        # --- Scaled dot product ---
   173→        acc = tl.dot_scaled(a_fp4, a_scale, "e2m1", b, b_scale, "e2m1", acc=acc)
   174→
   175→    # Store result
   176→    c = acc.to(tl.bfloat16)
   177→    tl.store(
   178→        C_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_cm + offs_n[None, :] * stride_cn,
   179→        c,
   180→        mask=mask_m[:, None] & mask_n[None, :],
   181→    )
   182→
   183→
   184→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   185→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
   186→    m, k = A.shape
   187→    n = B.shape[0]
   188→    k_scale = k // 32
   189→
   190→    try:
   191→        from tritonblas import OrigamiMatmulSelector
   192→
   193→        # Unshuffle B scale
   194→        B_scale = e8m0_unshuffle(B_scale_sh.view(torch.uint8), orig_m=n, orig_n=k_scale)
   195→
   196→        # Transpose B for kernel (B_q is [N, K//2], kernel needs [K//2, N])
   197→        B_t = B_q.view(torch.uint8).t().contiguous()
   198→
   199→        # Use Origami selector for tile sizes
   200→        selector = OrigamiMatmulSelector(m, n, k, "f4", "f4", torch.bfloat16, A.device, mx_block_size=32)
   201→        BLOCK_M = max(selector.block_m, 16)  # min 16 for gfx950
   202→        BLOCK_N = selector.block_n
   203→        BLOCK_K = selector.block_k  # in fp4/bf16 elements
   204→        GROUP_SIZE_M = selector.group_m
   205→        NUM_XCDS = selector.num_sms
   206→
   207→        # Override if blocks are too small for matrix
   208→        if BLOCK_M < m:
   209→            BLOCK_M = 128
   210→        if BLOCK_N < n:
   211→            BLOCK_N = 128
   212→        if BLOCK_K < k:
   213→            BLOCK_K = 128
   214→
   215→        # BLOCK_K is in BF16 elements; packed bytes = BLOCK_K//2
   216→        # tl.dot_scaled requires packed_K >= 64 bytes, so BF16 K >= 128
   217→        BLOCK_K = max(BLOCK_K, 128)
   218→        if BLOCK_K % 128 != 0:
   219→            BLOCK_K = ((BLOCK_K + 127) // 128) * 128
   220→
   221→        total_blocks_M = triton.cdiv(m, BLOCK_M)
   222→        total_blocks_N = triton.cdiv(n, BLOCK_N)
   223→        total_tiles = total_blocks_M * total_blocks_N
   224→
   225→        CHUNK_SIZE = GROUP_SIZE_M * GROUP_SIZE_M
   226→        CHUNK_SIZE = min(CHUNK_SIZE, max(1, total_tiles // NUM_XCDS))
   227→
   228→        C = torch.empty(m, n, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=A.device)
   229→
   230→        grid = (total_tiles,)
   231→
   232→        print(f"FUSED: m={m}, n={n}, k={k}, BM={BLOCK_M}, BN={BLOCK_N}, BK={BLOCK_K}, "
   233→              f"GSM={GROUP_SIZE_M}, XCDS={NUM_XCDS}, tiles={total_tiles}", file=sys.stderr)
   234→
   235→        _fused_quant_gemm_kernel[grid](
   236→            A,
   237→            B_t,
   238→            C,
   239→            B_scale,
   240→            m, n, k,
   241→            A.stride(0), A.stride(1),
   242→            B_t.stride(0), B_t.stride(1),
   243→            B_scale.stride(0), B_scale.stride(1),
   244→            C.stride(0), C.stride(1),
   245→            BLOCK_M=BLOCK_M,
   246→            BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N,
   247→            BLOCK_K=BLOCK_K,
   248→            GROUP_SIZE_M=GROUP_SIZE_M,
   249→            NUM_XCDS=NUM_XCDS,
   250→            CHUNK_SIZE=CHUNK_SIZE,
   251→            SCALE_GROUP_SIZE=32,
   252→            num_warps=8,
   253→            num_stages=2,
   254→        )
   255→        return C
   256→
   257→    except Exception as e:
   258→        print(f"FUSED KERNEL ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   259→        import traceback
   260→        traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)
   261→        # Fallback
   262→        from reference import ref_kernel
   263→        return ref_kernel(data)
   264→
```

```
     1→"""Test Helion code generation for MXFP4 GEMM."""
     2→import helion
     3→import helion.language as hl
     4→import torch
     5→
     6→# Try MXFP4 scaled GEMM first
     7→@helion.kernel(settings=helion.Settings(print_output_code=True))
     8→def mxfp4_gemm(
     9→    A_q: torch.Tensor,       # (M, K//2) uint8 packed fp4
    10→    A_scale: torch.Tensor,   # (M, K//32) e8m0 scales
    11→    B_q: torch.Tensor,       # (K//2, N) uint8 packed fp4
    12→    B_scale: torch.Tensor,   # (K//32, N) e8m0 scales
    13→) -> torch.Tensor:
    14→    M = A_q.shape[0]
    15→    N = B_q.shape[1]
    16→    out = torch.empty([M, N], dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=A_q.device)
    17→    for tile_m, tile_n in hl.tile([M, N]):
    18→        acc = hl.zeros([tile_m, tile_n], dtype=torch.float32)
    19→        for tile_k in hl.tile(A_q.shape[1]):
    20→            acc = hl.dot_scaled(
    21→                A_q[tile_m, tile_k], A_scale[tile_m, :], "e2m1",
    22→                B_q[tile_k, tile_n], B_scale[:, tile_n], "e2m1",
    23→                acc=acc,
    24→            )
    25→        out[tile_m, tile_n] = acc.to(torch.bfloat16)
    26→    return out
    27→
    28→
    29→# Also try a simple bf16 GEMM for baseline
    30→@helion.kernel(settings=helion.Settings(print_output_code=True))
    31→def bf16_gemm(x: torch.Tensor, y: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    32→    out = torch.empty([x.shape[0], y.shape[1]], dtype=x.dtype, device=x.device)
    33→    for tile_m, tile_n in hl.tile([out.shape[0], out.shape[1]]):
    34→        acc = hl.zeros([tile_m, tile_n], dtype=torch.float32)
    35→        for tile_k in hl.tile(x.shape[1]):
    36→            acc = hl.dot(x[tile_m, tile_k], y[tile_k, tile_n], acc)
    37→        out[tile_m, tile_n] = acc.to(out.dtype)
    38→    return out
    39→
    40→
    41→if __name__ == "__main__":
    42→    print("=== Attempting bf16 GEMM bind ===")
    43→    try:
    44→        a = torch.empty(256, 512, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device='cpu')
    45→        b = torch.empty(512, 1024, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device='cpu')
    46→        bound = bf16_gemm.bind((a, b))
    47→        print(f"Bound type: {type(bound)}")
    48→        print(f"Bound attrs: {[x for x in dir(bound) if not x.startswith('_')]}")
    49→
    50→        # Try to extract generated code
    51→        for attr in ['source_code', 'triton_code', 'code', 'output_code']:
    52→            if hasattr(bound, attr):
    53→                print(f"\n=== {attr} ===")
    54→                print(getattr(bound, attr))
    55→    except Exception as e:
    56→        print(f"bf16 error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
    57→
    58→    print("\n=== Attempting MXFP4 GEMM bind ===")
    59→    try:
    60→        aq = torch.empty(256, 256, dtype=torch.uint8, device='cpu')  # K=512 packed → 256
    61→        asc = torch.empty(256, 16, dtype=torch.uint8, device='cpu')   # K/32=16
    62→        bq = torch.empty(256, 1024, dtype=torch.uint8, device='cpu')
    63→        bsc = torch.empty(16, 1024, dtype=torch.uint8, device='cpu')
    64→        bound = mxfp4_gemm.bind((aq, asc, bq, bsc))
    65→        print(f"Bound type: {type(bound)}")
    66→        print(f"Bound attrs: {[x for x in dir(bound) if not x.startswith('_')]}")
    67→    except Exception as e:
    68→        print(f"mxfp4 error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
    69→
```

```
     1→"""MXFP4 GEMM via tritonblas.matmul_fp4 — zero B re-quantization.
     2→
     3→Reverses e8m0_shuffle to recover un-shuffled B_scale from B_scale_sh.
     4→e8m0_shuffle:
     5→  pad M→ceil(256), N→ceil(8)
     6→  view(M//32, 2, 16, N//8, 2, 4)
     7→  permute(0, 3, 5, 2, 4, 1)
     8→  view(M_pad, N_pad)
     9→Inverse:
    10→  view(M//32, N//8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
    11→  permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2)
    12→  view(M_pad, N_pad)
    13→  strip padding → [M_orig, N_orig]
    14→"""
    15→import torch
    16→from task import input_t, output_t
    17→from tritonblas import matmul_fp4
    18→from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    19→
    20→
    21→def e8m0_unshuffle(scale_shuffled: torch.Tensor, orig_m: int, orig_n: int) -> torch.Tensor:
    22→    """Reverse e8m0_shuffle: recover original [orig_m, orig_n] scale."""
    23→    sm, sn = scale_shuffled.shape
    24→    # The shuffled tensor has padded dims: sm = ceil(orig_m/256)*256, sn = ceil(orig_n/8)*8
    25→    # After shuffle, layout is: view(sm//32, sn//8, 4, 16, 2, 2) from permute(0,3,5,2,4,1)
    26→    # Inverse permute of (0,3,5,2,4,1) is (0,5,3,1,4,2)
    27→    scale = scale_shuffled.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
    28→    scale = scale.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()
    29→    scale = scale.view(sm, sn)
    30→    return scale[:orig_m, :orig_n]
    31→
    32→
    33→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    34→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    35→
    36→    m, k = A.shape
    37→    n = B.shape[0]
    38→
    39→    # Quantize A
    40→    A_fp4, A_scale = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())
    41→
    42→    # B: reuse B_q data, UNSHUFFLE B_scale_sh to get un-shuffled scale
    43→    # B_scale_sh is [N_padded, K_scale_padded] in e8m0 shuffled format
    44→    # Original B_scale was [N, K//32] before shuffle
    45→    k_scale = k // 32  # un-shuffled scale K dimension
    46→    B_scale = e8m0_unshuffle(
    47→        B_scale_sh.view(torch.uint8),
    48→        orig_m=n,         # B_scale original M = N (number of rows in B)
    49→        orig_n=k_scale,   # B_scale original N = K//32
    50→    )
    51→
    52→    C = torch.empty(m, n, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=A.device)
    53→
    54→    matmul_fp4(
    55→        A_fp4.view(torch.uint8),
    56→        B_q.view(torch.uint8),
    57→        C,
    58→        A_scale.view(torch.uint8),
    59→        B_scale,
    60→    )
    61→
    62→    return C
    63→
```

Good, clean slate. Here's the plan based on what I've found:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Phase 3 Strategy — Why this order matters:**
1. The **fused kernel** (`submission_tritonblas_fused.py`) was written but NEVER submitted. It eliminates the separate `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` kernel launch (~10µs). This is the highest-impact change.
2. But fusing quantization into the GEMM is risky — the manual E8M0 scale formula may not match what `tl.dot_scaled` expects. So we first test a **pre-quantized tl.dot_scaled kernel with Origami scheduling** to validate the kernel structure.
3. If the pre-quant kernel works but isn't faster, the fused version removes the quant overhead and could close the gap from 20.6µs toward the 9.7µs leader.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
