{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/.claude/skills/deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap/SKILL.md",
  "content": "---\nname: deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap\ndescription: |\n  Architectural analysis and optimization strategy for DeepSeek MLA decode on AMD MI355X.\n  Use when: (1) MLA decode benchmark shows >5x gap to leaderboard leaders with correct output,\n  (2) aiter 3-stage pipeline overhead dominates small-batch decode timing (~100-150 µs constant),\n  (3) planning a custom Triton or FlashInfer-based MLA attention kernel,\n  (4) trying to replicate or understand competition leader approaches (4.3 µs on MI355X).\n  Key insight: The 22.9x gap is ALGORITHMIC — the 3-stage aiter pipeline has fixed metadata\n  overhead that exceeds the actual compute for small batches. Flash Attention-style fused\n  tiling eliminates this overhead but requires handling MLA's K≠V head dimension.\nauthor: Claude Code\nversion: 1.0.0\ntags: [amd, mi355x, mla, deepseek, flash-attention, attention, gqa, triton, aiter]\n---\n\n# DeepSeek MLA Decode: Flash Attention Architectural Gap\n\n## Problem\n\nThe aiter `mla_decode_fwd` 3-stage pipeline has ~100-150 µs fixed overhead regardless of\nbatch size. For small decode workloads (bs ≤ 64), the actual attention compute is <10 µs —\nthe pipeline overhead is the bottleneck, NOT the compute.\n\nOur best hybrid approach (torch.einsum for small batches) achieves ~97 µs geomean on MI355X.\nCompetition leaders achieve **4.3 µs** — a 22.9x gap that cannot be closed by parameter tuning.\n\n## Architectural Context: DeepSeek R1 MLA\n\nMLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) uses an **absorbed** formulation:\n- Q: `(total_q, num_heads, 576)` — already has rope/projection absorbed (QK head dim = 576)\n- KV: `(total_kv, 1, 576)` — single KV head, fused K and V into one latent buffer\n- V is sliced from KV: `V = KV[:, :, :512]` (first 512 of 576 dims)\n- Output: `(total_q, num_heads, 512)` — value head dim = 512\n\nThis is GQA with 1 KV head and 16-32 Q heads (128 total // tensor parallelism 4-8).\n\n### Key Dimensions (DeepSeek R1, TP=4)\n\n```python\nNUM_HEADS = 32        # 128 // TP=4\nNUM_KV_HEADS = 1      # MLA always has 1 KV head\nQK_HEAD_DIM = 576     # for score computation: Q @ KV^T\nV_HEAD_DIM = 512      # for value: KV[:, :, :512]\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / sqrt(576)  # attention temperature\n```\n\n## The 3-Stage Pipeline Overhead\n\naiter's `mla_decode_fwd` runs:\n1. `get_mla_metadata_v1()` — computes work distribution, fills 6 metadata tensors (~5-20 µs)\n2. `mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd()` — split-K attention kernel, produces partial results (~70-120 µs)\n3. `mla_decode_reduce_fwd()` — parallel reduce of partial results (~10-30 µs)\n\n**Total fixed overhead: ~100-150 µs.** For bs=4, kv=1024 (4096 KV tokens), the actual\nattention compute on 32 heads is trivial. The metadata + split-K + reduce dominate entirely.\n\n## What the 4.3 µs Leaders Are Likely Doing\n\nLeaders (Jayluci4, n8_gr8_ at 4.3 µs on MI355X) almost certainly use a **fused Flash\nAttention-style kernel** that:\n\n1. Processes Q in tiles (BLOCK_M), iterates over KV in tiles (BLOCK_N)\n2. Fuses score, softmax, and value accumulation into a SINGLE kernel (Flash Attention v2 pattern)\n3. Eliminates the 3-stage pipeline overhead entirely — one kernel launch per call\n4. Handles GQA by broadcasting the 1 KV head across all 32 Q heads within the kernel\n\nFor bs=4, kv=1024: `4 * 1 * ceil(32/GROUP) * ceil(1024/BLOCK_N)` = a handful of thread blocks.\nA single kernel launch at ~4 µs is consistent with this.\n\n## Critical Layout Constraint: K ≠ V Head Dimension\n\nMLA's fused KV buffer has:\n- K dimension: 576 (for score: `Q @ KV^T` uses full 576)\n- V dimension: 512 (for value: `@V` uses only first 512 of each KV row)\n\n**Standard Flash Attention assumes K_dim == V_dim.** `fav3_sage_mxfp4` and other standard\nattention kernels FAIL here because they expect separate K, V tensors with the same head_dim.\n\nAny custom Flash Attention for MLA must:\n```python\n# Score computation: use full KV dim (576)\nscores = tl.dot(q_tile, kv_tile.T)  # q_tile: [BM, 576], kv_tile: [BN, 576]\n\n# Value extraction: slice first 512 dims of KV\nv_tile = kv_tile[:, :512]           # [BN, 512]\nacc += tl.dot(weights, v_tile)      # acc: [BM, 512]\n```\n\n## Available Packages to Check on MI355X Runner\n\nBefore writing a custom kernel, probe for pre-built solutions:\n\n```python\n# Submit as probe via Popcorn CLI (--mode test, delegates to ref_kernel for correctness)\nimport sys\nfor pkg in [\"flash_attn\", \"flashinfer\", \"xformers\", \"vllm\"]:\n    try:\n        mod = __import__(pkg)\n        print(f\"FOUND: {pkg} {getattr(mod, '__version__', 'unknown')}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        # For flash_attn: check if it has varlen/GQA support\n        if pkg == \"flash_attn\":\n            from flash_attn import flash_attn_varlen_func\n            print(f\"  flash_attn_varlen_func: available\", file=sys.stderr)\n        if pkg == \"flashinfer\":\n            import flashinfer\n            print(f\"  flashinfer BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper: checking...\", file=sys.stderr)\n    except ImportError:\n        print(f\"NOT FOUND: {pkg}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n# Also check aiter's attention ops\nimport os, aiter\naiter_path = os.path.dirname(aiter.__file__)\nfor root, dirs, files in os.walk(os.path.join(aiter_path, \"ops\", \"triton\", \"attention\")):\n    for f in sorted(files):\n        print(f\"ATTN: {f}\", file=sys.stderr)\n```\n\n## Custom Triton Flash Attention for MLA (If No Library Available)\n\nIf no library is available, write a Flash Attention v2 variant:\n\n```python\n@triton.jit\ndef mla_decode_flash_kernel(\n    Q_ptr, KV_ptr, O_ptr,\n    # strides, shapes...\n    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr, BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,\n    QK_DIM: tl.constexpr,  # 576\n    V_DIM: tl.constexpr,   # 512\n    SM_SCALE: tl.constexpr,\n):\n    # Grid: (num_q_heads * batch, cdiv(total_q, BLOCK_M))\n    # Each thread block processes BLOCK_M query positions\n\n    m_i = tl.full([BLOCK_M], float(\"-inf\"), dtype=tl.float32)  # running max\n    l_i = tl.zeros([BLOCK_M], dtype=tl.float32)                # running sum\n    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_M, V_DIM], dtype=tl.float32)         # accumulator\n\n    for kv_start in range(0, total_kv, BLOCK_N):\n        kv_tile = tl.load(KV_ptr + kv_start * stride_kv ...)   # [BN, 576]\n\n        # Score: full QK dim\n        qk = tl.dot(q, kv_tile.T) * SM_SCALE   # [BM, BN]\n\n        # Flash Attention safe-softmax update\n        m_new = tl.maximum(m_i, tl.max(qk, 1))\n        alpha = tl.exp(m_i - m_new)\n        p = tl.exp(qk - m_new[:, None])\n        l_i = alpha * l_i + tl.sum(p, 1)\n        acc = alpha[:, None] * acc + tl.dot(p, kv_tile[:, :V_DIM])  # V is first 512 dims\n        m_i = m_new\n\n    # Normalize\n    acc = acc / l_i[:, None]\n    tl.store(O_ptr + ...)\n```\n\nKey: `kv_tile[:, :V_DIM]` extracts V from the fused KV buffer without extra memory allocation.\n\n## Performance Targets (MI355X Benchmark Shapes)\n\n| Shape | Our hybrid | Target (Flash Attention) | Leader |\n|-------|-----------|--------------------------|--------|\n| bs=4, kv=1k | 27.7 µs | ~4-8 µs | 4.3 µs |\n| bs=4, kv=8k | 40.5 µs | ~8-15 µs | ~4-8 µs |\n| bs=32, kv=1k | 46.2 µs | ~8-20 µs | ~4-10 µs |\n| bs=256, kv=8k | 399 µs | ~100-200 µs | unknown |\n| **Geomean** | ~97 µs | ~15-40 µs | ~4.3 µs |\n\nA simple non-persistent Flash Attention would likely achieve 15-40 µs geomean.\nA persistent kernel with Origami scheduling could approach 4-10 µs for small batches.\n\n## Implementation Priority\n\n1. **First: Probe for flash_attn, flashinfer** — 1 submission, zero implementation\n2. **If flashinfer found:** Use `BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper` with custom head dims\n3. **If flash_attn found:** Use `flash_attn_varlen_func` with GQA support\n4. **If nothing found:** Write custom Triton Flash Attention kernel (~100 lines)\n\nThe custom Triton kernel is entirely feasible and would be the most interesting.\nUse Helion locally (`~/.venv-helion/bin/python`) to generate scaffold code.\n\n## Context\n\nAnalyzed during Luma AMD Speedrun competition (March 2026, AMD MI355X).\nCompetition deadline: 2026-03-30. Leaders at 4.3 µs (Jayluci4, n8_gr8_).\nOur best: ~97 µs (torch.einsum hybrid). Runner: gfx950, ROCm 7.1.\nSee also: `competitive-kernel-optimization-ceiling`, `aiter-mxfp4-api-limitations`.\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode — Discovery probe: check for flash_attn, flashinfer, vllm, xformers\nand list all aiter attention ops available on MI355X runner.\nFalls back to hybrid torch-native for correctness.\n\"\"\"\nimport sys\nimport os\nimport torch\nfrom reference import ref_kernel\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)\nTORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000\n\n_probed = False\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    global _probed\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    if not _probed:\n        _probed = True\n\n        # 1. Check for attention libraries\n        for pkg in [\"flash_attn\", \"flashinfer\", \"xformers\", \"vllm\",\n                     \"triton_flash_attention\", \"flash_attention\"]:\n            try:\n                mod = __import__(pkg)\n                ver = getattr(mod, \"__version__\", \"unknown\")\n                print(f\"FOUND: {pkg} v{ver}\", file=sys.stderr)\n                # Dig deeper into flash_attn\n                if pkg == \"flash_attn\":\n                    try:\n                        from flash_attn import flash_attn_varlen_func\n                        print(f\"  flash_attn_varlen_func: AVAILABLE\", file=sys.stderr)\n                    except ImportError as e:\n                        print(f\"  flash_attn_varlen_func: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n                    try:\n                        from flash_attn import flash_attn_func\n                        print(f\"  flash_attn_func: AVAILABLE\", file=sys.stderr)\n                    except ImportError as e:\n                        print(f\"  flash_attn_func: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n                # Dig deeper into flashinfer\n                if pkg == \"flashinfer\":\n                    try:\n                        from flashinfer import BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper\n                        print(f\"  BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper: AVAILABLE\",\n                              file=sys.stderr)\n                    except ImportError as e:\n                        print(f\"  BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper: {e}\",\n                              file=sys.stderr)\n                    try:\n                        from flashinfer import single_decode_with_kv_cache\n                        print(f\"  single_decode_with_kv_cache: AVAILABLE\",\n                              file=sys.stderr)\n                    except ImportError as e:\n                        print(f\"  single_decode_with_kv_cache: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n            except ImportError:\n                print(f\"NOT FOUND: {pkg}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n        # 2. List aiter attention-related ops and modules\n        try:\n            import aiter\n            aiter_path = os.path.dirname(aiter.__file__)\n\n            # Check triton attention directory\n            attn_dir = os.path.join(aiter_path, \"ops\", \"triton\", \"attention\")\n            if os.path.isdir(attn_dir):\n                print(f\"\\n=== aiter/ops/triton/attention/ ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n                for f in sorted(os.listdir(attn_dir)):\n                    fpath = os.path.join(attn_dir, f)\n                    size = os.path.getsize(fpath) if os.path.isfile(fpath) else 0\n                    print(f\"  {f} ({size} bytes)\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n            # Check for other attention modules\n            for subdir in [\"ops/hip\", \"ops/triton\", \"ops\"]:\n                d = os.path.join(aiter_path, subdir)\n                if os.path.isdir(d):\n                    for f in sorted(os.listdir(d)):\n                        if \"attn\" in f.lower() or \"mla\" in f.lower() or \"flash\" in f.lower():\n                            fpath = os.path.join(d, f)\n                            size = os.path.getsize(fpath) if os.path.isfile(fpath) else 0\n                            print(f\"  {subdir}/{f} ({size} bytes)\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n            # Check top-level aiter for attention-related callables\n            print(f\"\\n=== aiter top-level attention attrs ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n            for attr in sorted(dir(aiter)):\n                if any(k in attr.lower() for k in [\"attn\", \"mla\", \"flash\", \"decode\"]):\n                    obj = getattr(aiter, attr, None)\n                    print(f\"  aiter.{attr}: {type(obj).__name__}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"AITER SCAN ERROR: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n        # 3. Check for tritonblas attention ops\n        try:\n            import tritonblas\n            print(f\"\\n=== tritonblas exports ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n            for attr in sorted(dir(tritonblas)):\n                if not attr.startswith(\"_\"):\n                    print(f\"  tritonblas.{attr}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        except ImportError:\n            print(f\"tritonblas: NOT FOUND\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n        # 4. Check pip list for attention-related packages\n        try:\n            import subprocess\n            result = subprocess.run(\n                [\"pip\", \"list\", \"--format=columns\"],\n                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10,\n            )\n            print(f\"\\n=== Attention-related pip packages ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n            for line in result.stdout.split(\"\\n\"):\n                ll = line.lower()\n                if any(k in ll for k in [\"flash\", \"attn\", \"attention\", \"flashinfer\",\n                                          \"xformers\", \"vllm\", \"triton\", \"ck\", \"composable\"]):\n                    print(f\"  {line.strip()}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"PIP LIST ERROR: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # Fall back to hybrid for correctness\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]\n    total_q = q.shape[0]\n    qseqlen = total_q // bs\n\n    if qseqlen != 1 or bs * kvseqlen > TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD:\n        return ref_kernel(data)\n\n    kv = kv_data[\"bf16\"].view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)\n    scores = torch.einsum(\"bnh,bsh->bns\", q, kv) * SM_SCALE\n    weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)\n    v = kv[:, :, :dv]\n    out = torch.einsum(\"bns,bsd->bnd\", weights, v)\n    return out\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"GEMM — gemm_afp4wfp4 Triton kernel with auto-tuning (20.6 us geomean, 2.12x leader gap).\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import dtypes\nfrom aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant\nfrom aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4 import gemm_afp4wfp4\n\n\ndef _e8m0_unshuffle(scale_sh: torch.Tensor, orig_m: int, orig_n: int) -> torch.Tensor:\n    u8 = scale_sh.view(torch.uint8)\n    sm, sn = u8.shape\n    s = u8.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)\n    s = s.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()\n    return s.view(sm, sn)[:orig_m, :orig_n]\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n    A_fp4, A_scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)\n    n, k = B.shape\n    B_scale = _e8m0_unshuffle(B_scale_sh, orig_m=n, orig_n=k // 32)\n    return gemm_afp4wfp4(\n        A_fp4.view(torch.uint8), B_q.view(torch.uint8),\n        A_scale_e8m0.view(torch.uint8), B_scale.contiguous().view(torch.uint8),\n        dtype=dtypes.bf16,\n    )\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMXFP4 MoE — Phase 10 baseline + fmoe_g1u1 1-stage probe.\n\nProbes whether the gated-off 1-stage kernel (fmoe_g1u1) is callable directly.\nFalls back to proven Phase 10 adaptive KSPLIT on failure.\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType\n\n_probed = False\n_g1u1_available = False\n_g1u1_func = None\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    global _probed, _g1u1_available, _g1u1_func\n    (\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight,\n        down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale,\n        down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        config,\n    ) = data\n\n    if not _probed:\n        _probed = True\n        try:\n            # 1. Check if fmoe_g1u1 is importable\n            import aiter\n            if hasattr(aiter, \"fmoe_g1u1\"):\n                print(f\"FOUND: aiter.fmoe_g1u1\", file=sys.stderr)\n                _g1u1_func = aiter.fmoe_g1u1\n                _g1u1_available = True\n\n                # Print signature\n                import inspect\n                sig = inspect.signature(_g1u1_func)\n                print(f\"  signature: {sig}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n                # Print source (first 40 lines)\n                try:\n                    src = inspect.getsource(_g1u1_func)\n                    for i, line in enumerate(src.split(\"\\n\")[:40]):\n                        print(f\"  {line}\", file=sys.stderr)\n                except Exception as e:\n                    print(f\"  source error: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n            else:\n                print(f\"NOT FOUND: aiter.fmoe_g1u1\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n            # 2. Check fused_moe_1stage_dict\n            try:\n                from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe_1stage_dict\n                print(f\"\\n=== fused_moe_1stage_dict ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n                for platform, entries in fused_moe_1stage_dict.items():\n                    print(f\"  Platform: {platform}\", file=sys.stderr)\n                    for key, func in entries.items():\n                        print(f\"    {key} -> {func.__name__}\", file=sys.stderr)\n            except ImportError as e:\n                print(f\"fused_moe_1stage_dict import error: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n            # 3. Check for other MoE-related functions\n            print(f\"\\n=== aiter MoE attrs ===\", file=sys.stderr)\n            for attr in sorted(dir(aiter)):\n                if any(k in attr.lower() for k in [\"moe\", \"fmoe\", \"expert\"]):\n                    obj = getattr(aiter, attr, None)\n                    print(f\"  aiter.{attr}: {type(obj).__name__}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n            # 4. Check for moe_sorting variants\n            for name in [\"moe_sorting_fwd\", \"moe_sorting_opus_fwd\",\n                         \"moe_sorting_fwd_v2\"]:\n                if hasattr(aiter, name):\n                    print(f\"  SORT: aiter.{name} available\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"PROBE ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n            import traceback\n            traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # --- Production path: Phase 10 adaptive KSPLIT ---\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]\n    estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts\n\n    if estimated_m >= 50:\n        os.environ.pop(\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\", None)\n        os.environ.pop(\"AITER_KSPLIT\", None)\n    elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:\n        os.environ[\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\"] = \"1\"\n        os.environ[\"AITER_KSPLIT\"] = \"4\"\n    else:\n        os.environ[\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\"] = \"1\"\n        os.environ[\"AITER_KSPLIT\"] = \"2\"\n\n    return fused_moe(\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        expert_mask=None,\n        activation=ActivationType.Silu,\n        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,\n        doweight_stage1=False,\n        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        a1_scale=None,\n        a2_scale=None,\n        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,\n    )\n"
}

### Apply Attention with Paged KV Cache using XQA MLA Kernel

Source: https://docs.flashinfer.ai/api/attention

Applies attention with a paged KV cache using the XQA MLA (Multi-Head Latent Attention) kernel. It supports query, key cache, value cache, page table, sequence lengths, output, workspace buffer, semaphores, page size, scale factors, SM count, and PDL enablement. Currently, only beam_width 1 is supported for the query tensor.

```python
xqa_mla(q, k_cache, v_cache, page_table, seq_lens, output, workspace_buffer, semaphores, page_size, q_scale=1.0, kv_scale=1.0, sm_count=None, enable_pdl=None)
```

--------------------------------

### TRTLLM Batch Decode with KV Cache using MLA (Python)

Source: https://docs.flashinfer.ai/generated/flashinfer.mla.trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache_mla

The `trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache_mla` function performs batched decoding using the Multi-Layer Attention (MLA) mechanism with a KV cache. It supports various configurations for head dimensions, LoRA rank, and scaling, and can utilize different backends like 'auto', 'xqa', or 'trtllm-gen'. The function requires several tensor inputs, including query, KV cache, workspace buffer, block tables, and sequence lengths, along with integer parameters for dimensions and lengths.

```python
flashinfer.mla.trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache_mla(
    _query : Tensor_,
    _kv_cache : Tensor_,
    _workspace_buffer : Tensor_,
    _qk_nope_head_dim : int_,
    _kv_lora_rank : int_,
    _qk_rope_head_dim : int_,
    _block_tables : Tensor_,
    _seq_lens : Tensor_,
    _max_seq_len : int_,
    _sparse_mla_top_k : int = 0_,
    _out : Tensor | None = None_,
    _bmm1_scale : float | Tensor = 1.0_,
    _bmm2_scale : float | Tensor = 1.0_,
    _sinks : List[Tensor] | None = None_,
    _enable_pdl : bool = None_,
    _backend : str = 'auto'_ 
) → Tensor
```

### flashinfer.xqa.xqa_mla

Source: https://docs.flashinfer.ai/generated/flashinfer.xqa.xqa_mla

Applies attention with a paged KV cache using the XQA MLA (Multi-Head Latent Attention) kernel. This function modifies the output tensor in-place.

```APIDOC
## POST /flashinfer/xqa/xqa_mla

### Description
Applies attention with a paged KV cache using the XQA MLA (Multi-Head Latent Attention) kernel. This function modifies the output tensor in-place.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/flashinfer/xqa/xqa_mla

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **q** (torch.Tensor) - Query tensor with shape `[batch_size, beam_width, num_q_heads, head_dim]`. Data type should be torch.float8_e4m3fn. Now only beam_width 1 is supported.
- **k_cache** (torch.Tensor) - Paged K cache tensor with shape `[total_num_cache_heads, head_dim]`. Data type should be torch.float8_e4m3fn
- **v_cache** (torch.Tensor) - Paged V cache tensor with shape `[total_num_cache_heads, head_dim]`. Data type should be torch.float8_e4m3fn
- **page_table** (torch.Tensor) - Page table tensor with shape `batch_size, nb_pages_per_seq`. Data type should be torch.int32. K and V share the same table.
- **seq_lens** (torch.Tensor) - Sequence lengths tensor with shape `[batch_size, beam_width]`. Data type should be torch.uint32.
- **output** (torch.Tensor) - Output tensor with shape `[batch_size, beam_width, num_q_heads, head_dim]`. Data type should be torch.bfloat16. This tensor will be modified in-place.
- **workspace_buffer** (torch.Tensor) - Workspace buffer for temporary computations. Data type should be torch.uint8.
- **semaphores** (torch.Tensor) - Semaphore buffer for synchronization. Data type should be torch.uint32.
- **page_size** (int) - Size of each page in the paged KV cache. Must be one of [16, 32, 64, 128].
- **q_scale** (Union[float, torch.Tensor], optional) - Scale factor for query tensor. Defaults to 1.0.
- **kv_scale** (Union[float, torch.Tensor], optional) - Scale factor for KV cache. Defaults to 1.0.
- **sm_count** (Optional[int], optional) - Number of streaming multiprocessors to use. If None, will be inferred from the device. Defaults to None.
- **enable_pdl** (Optional[bool], optional) - Whether to enable PDL (Persistent Data Loader) optimization. If None, will be set to True if hardware supports it. Defaults to None.

Note:
    The function automatically infers several parameters from tensor shapes:
    - batch_size from q.shape[0]
    - head_dim from q.shape[-1]
    - head_group_ratio is fixed to 128 for MLA
    - max_seq_len from page_table.shape[-1] * page_size

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **None** - The function modifies the output tensor in-place and returns None.
```

### XQA Kernel Function for Paged KV Cache Attention

Source: https://docs.flashinfer.ai/api/attention

The xqa function applies attention with a paged KV cache. It supports various configurations including FP8 calculations, attention sinks, and speculative decoding. Ensure input tensors have compatible data types and shapes as specified.

```python
def xqa(q: torch.Tensor, k_cache: torch.Tensor, v_cache: torch.Tensor, page_table: torch.Tensor, seq_lens: torch.Tensor, output: torch.Tensor, workspace_buffer: torch.Tensor, semaphores: torch.Tensor, num_kv_heads: int, page_size: int, sinks: Optional[torch.Tensor] = None, q_scale: Union[float, torch.Tensor] = 1.0, kv_scale: Union[float, torch.Tensor] = 1.0, sliding_win_size: int = 0, kv_layout: str = "NHD", sm_count: Optional[int] = None, enable_pdl: Optional[bool] = None, rcp_out_scale: float = 1.0, q_seq_len: int = 1, mask: Optional[torch.Tensor] = None):
    """Apply attention with paged KV cache using XQA kernel.

    :param q: Query tensor with shape `[batch_size, beam_width, num_q_heads, head_dim]` if not using speculative decoding, or `[batch_size, beam_width, q_seq_len, num_q_heads, head_dim]` if using speculative decoding. `q_seq_len` is the number of speculative decoding tokens. Data type should be torch.float16 or torch.bfloat16. Now only beam_width 1 is supported.
    :type q: torch.Tensor
    :param k_cache: Paged K cache tensor with shape `[num_pages, page_size, num_kv_heads, head_dim]` if `kv_layout` is `NHD`, or `[num_pages, num_kv_heads, page_size, head_dim]` if `kv_layout` is `HND`. Data type should match query tensor or be torch.float8_e4m3fn, in which case xqa will run fp8 calculation. Should be the same data type as v_cache.
    :type k_cache: torch.Tensor
    :param v_cache: Paged V cache tensor with shape `[num_pages, page_size, num_kv_heads, head_dim]` if `kv_layout` is `NHD`, or `[num_pages, num_kv_heads, page_size, head_dim]` if `kv_layout` is `HND`. Data type should match query tensor or be torch.float8_e4m3fn, in which case xqa will run fp8 calculation. Should be the same data type as k_cache.
    :type v_cache: torch.Tensor
    :param page_table: Page table tensor with shape `batch_size, nb_pages_per_seq`. Data type should be torch.int32. K and V share the same table.
    :type page_table: torch.Tensor
    :param seq_lens: Sequence lengths tensor with shape `[batch_size, beam_width]`. Data type should be torch.uint32.
    :type seq_lens: torch.Tensor
    :param output: Output tensor with shape that matches the query tensor. Data type should match query tensor or kv tensor. This tensor will be modified in-place.
    :type output: torch.Tensor
    :param workspace_buffer: Workspace buffer for temporary computations. Data type should be torch.uint8.
    :type workspace_buffer: torch.Tensor
    :param semaphores: Semaphore buffer for synchronization. Data type should be torch.uint32.
    :type semaphores: torch.Tensor
    :param num_kv_heads: Number of key-value heads in the attention mechanism.
    :type num_kv_heads: int
    :param page_size: Size of each page in the paged KV cache. Must be one of [16, 32, 64, 128].
    :type page_size: int
    :param sinks: Attention sink values with shape `[num_kv_heads, head_group_ratio]`. Data type should be torch.float32. If None, no attention sinks are used.
    :type sinks: Optional[torch.Tensor], default=None
    :param q_scale: Scale factor for query tensor.
    :type q_scale: Union[float, torch.Tensor], default=1.0
    :param kv_scale: Scale factor for KV cache.
    :type kv_scale: Union[float, torch.Tensor], default=1.0
    :param sliding_win_size: Sliding window size for attention. If 0, no sliding window is used.
    :type sliding_win_size: int, default=0
    :param kv_layout: The layout of the KV cache. Can be either `NHD` or `HND`.
    :type kv_layout: str, default="NHD"
    :param sm_count: Number of streaming multiprocessors to use. If None, will be inferred from the device.
    :type sm_count: Optional[int], default=None
    :param enable_pdl: Whether to enable PDL (Persistent Data Loader) optimization. If None, will be set to True if hardware supports it.
    :type enable_pdl: Optional[bool], default=None
    :param rcp_out_scale: Reciprocal of output scale factor.
    :type rcp_out_scale: float, default=1.0
    :param q_seq_len: Query sequence length. When > 1, enables speculative decoding mode.
    :type q_seq_len: int, default=1
    :param mask: Causal attention mask for speculative decoding mode (when `q_seq_len > 1`). Shape: `[batch_size, q_seq_len, mask_size_per_row]` where `mask_size_per_row = ((q_seq_len + 31) // 32) * 2`. Data type should be torch.uint16 (bit-packed format, aligned to 32 bits).
    :type mask: Optional[torch.Tensor], default=None.
    """
    pass  # Placeholder for the actual kernel implementation

```

### BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper

Source: https://docs.flashinfer.ai/api/attention

Wrapper class for batch decode attention with paged KV cache, designed for efficient processing of multiple requests.

```APIDOC
## BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper

### Description
Wrapper class for decode attention with paged kv-cache (first proposed in vLLM) for batch of requests. Check our tutorial for page table layout.

### Class Initialization
```python
flashinfer.BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper(
    workspace_buffer : torch.Tensor,
    kv_layout : str = 'NHD',
    use_cuda_graph : bool = False,
    use_tensor_cores : bool = False,
    paged_kv_indptr_buffer : Optional[torch.Tensor] = None,
    paged_kv_indices_buffer : Optional[torch.Tensor] = None,
    paged_kv_last_page_len_buffer : Optional[torch.Tensor] = None,
    backend : str = 'auto',
    jit_args : Optional[List[Any]] = None
)
```

### Parameters
#### Constructor Parameters
- **workspace_buffer** (torch.Tensor) - Required - The user reserved float workspace buffer used to store intermediate attention results in the split-k algorithm. The recommended size is 128MB, the device of the workspace buffer should be the same as the device of the input tensors.
- **kv_layout** (str) - Optional - The layout of the input k/v tensors, could be either `NHD` or `HND`. Defaults to 'NHD'.
- **use_cuda_graph** (bool) - Optional - Whether to enable CUDAGraph for batch decode attention. If enabled, the auxiliary data structures will be stored as the provided buffers. The `batch_size` cannot change during the lifecycle of this wrapper when CUDAGraph is enabled. Defaults to `False`.
- **use_tensor_cores** (bool) - Optional - Whether to use tensor cores for the computation. Will be faster for large group size in grouped query attention. Defaults to `False`.
- **paged_kv_indptr_buffer** (Optional[torch.Tensor]) - Optional - The user reserved buffer on GPU to store the indptr of the paged kv cache, the size of the buffer should be `[batch_size + 1]`. Only needed when `use_cuda_graph` is `True`.
- **paged_kv_indices_buffer** (Optional[torch.Tensor]) - Optional - The user reserved buffer on GPU to store the page indices of the paged kv cache, should be large enough to store the maximum number of page indices (`max_num_pages`) during the lifecycle of this wrapper. Only needed when `use_cuda_graph` is `True`.
- **paged_kv_last_page_len_buffer** (Optional[torch.Tensor]) - Optional - The user reserved buffer on GPU to store the number of entries in the last page, the size of the buffer should be `[batch_size]`. Only needed when `use_cuda_graph` is `True`.
- **backend** (str) - Optional - The backend to use for computation. Defaults to 'auto'.
- **jit_args** (Optional[List[Any]]) - Optional - Arguments for JIT compilation.

### Methods
#### plan
```python
decode_wrapper.plan(
    kv_page_indptr : torch.Tensor,
    kv_page_indices : torch.Tensor,
    kv_last_page_len : torch.Tensor,
    num_qo_heads : int,
    num_kv_heads : int,
    head_dim : int,
    page_size : int,
    pos_encoding_mode : str = 'NONE',
    data_type : torch.dtype = torch.float16
)
```

### Parameters
#### plan Parameters
- **kv_page_indptr** (torch.Tensor) - Required - The indptr of the paged KV cache.
- **kv_page_indices** (torch.Tensor) - Required - The page indices of the paged KV cache.
- **kv_last_page_len** (torch.Tensor) - Required - The number of entries in the last page.
- **num_qo_heads** (int) - Required - The number of query-only heads.
- **num_kv_heads** (int) - Required - The number of key-value heads.
- **head_dim** (int) - Required - The dimension of each head.
- **page_size** (int) - Required - The size of each page in the KV cache.
- **pos_encoding_mode** (str) - Optional - The position encoding mode. Defaults to 'NONE'.
- **data_type** (torch.dtype) - Optional - The data type for computation. Defaults to torch.float16.

#### run
```python
decode_wrapper.run(q : torch.Tensor, kv_cache : torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor
```

### Parameters
#### run Parameters
- **q** (torch.Tensor) - Required - The query tensor.
- **kv_cache** (torch.Tensor) - Required - The KV cache tensor for the current layer.

### Returns
- **torch.Tensor** - The output tensor after applying batch decode attention.

### Request Example
```python
>>> import torch
>>> import flashinfer
>>> num_layers = 32
>>> num_qo_heads = 64
>>> num_kv_heads = 8
>>> head_dim = 128
>>> max_num_pages = 128
>>> page_size = 16
>>> # allocate 128MB workspace buffer
>>> workspace_buffer = torch.zeros(128 * 1024 * 1024, dtype=torch.uint8, device="cuda:0")
>>> decode_wrapper = flashinfer.BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper(
...     workspace_buffer, "NHD"
... )
>>> batch_size = 7
>>> kv_page_indices = torch.arange(max_num_pages).int().to("cuda:0")
>>> kv_page_indptr = torch.tensor(
...     [0, 17, 29, 44, 48, 66, 100, 128], dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda:0"
... )
>>> # 1 <= kv_last_page_len <= page_size
>>> kv_last_page_len = torch.tensor(
...     [1, 7, 14, 4, 3, 1, 16], dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda:0"
... )
>>> kv_cache_at_layer = [
...     torch.randn(
...         max_num_pages, 2, page_size, num_kv_heads, head_dim, dtype=torch.float16, device="cuda:0"
...     ) for _ in range(num_layers)
... ]
>>> # create auxiliary data structures for batch decode attention
>>> decode_wrapper.plan(
...     kv_page_indptr,
...     kv_page_indices,
...     kv_last_page_len,
...     num_qo_heads,
...     num_kv_heads,
...     head_dim,
...     page_size,
...     pos_encoding_mode="NONE",
...     data_type=torch.float16
... )
>>> outputs = []
>>> for i in range(num_layers):
...     q = torch.randn(batch_size, num_qo_heads, head_dim).half().to("cuda:0")
...     kv_cache = kv_cache_at_layer[i]
...     # compute batch decode attention, reuse auxiliary data structures for all layers
...     o = decode_wrapper.run(q, kv_cache)
...     outputs.append(o)
...
>>> outputs[0].shape
torch.Size([7, 64, 128])

```

### Performing Flash Attention with Separate QKV in Python

Source: https://github.com/dao-ailab/flash-attention/blob/main/README.md

This function performs scaled dot product attention using separate Query, Key, and Value tensors. It supports multi-query and grouped-query attention (MQA/GQA) and features like dropout, softmax scaling, causal masking, sliding window attention, ALiBi slopes, and deterministic backward pass. Dropout should be 0.0 during evaluation.

```python
flash_attn_func(q, k, v, dropout_p=0.0, softmax_scale=None, causal=False,
                window_size=(-1, -1), alibi_slopes=None, deterministic=False):
"""dropout_p should be set to 0.0 during evaluation
Supports multi-query and grouped-query attention (MQA/GQA) by passing in KV with fewer heads
than Q. Note that the number of heads in Q must be divisible by the number of heads in KV.
For example, if Q has 6 heads and K, V have 2 heads, head 0, 1, 2 of Q will attention to head
0 of K, V, and head 3, 4, 5 of Q will attention to head 1 of K, V.
If window_size != (-1, -1), implements sliding window local attention. Query at position i
will only attend to keys between
[i + seqlen_k - seqlen_q - window_size[0], i + seqlen_k - seqlen_q + window_size[1]] inclusive.

Arguments:
    q: (batch_size, seqlen, nheads, headdim)
    k: (batch_size, seqlen, nheads_k, headdim)
    v: (batch_size, seqlen, nheads_k, headdim)
    dropout_p: float. Dropout probability.
    softmax_scale: float. The scaling of QK^T before applying softmax.
        Default to 1 / sqrt(headdim).
    causal: bool. Whether to apply causal attention mask (e.g., for auto-regressive modeling).
    window_size: (left, right). If not (-1, -1), implements sliding window local attention.
    alibi_slopes: (nheads,) or (batch_size, nheads), fp32. A bias of
        (-alibi_slope * |i + seqlen_k - seqlen_q - j|)
        is added to the attention score of query i and key j.
    deterministic: bool. Whether to use the deterministic implementation of the backward pass,
        which is slightly slower and uses more memory. The forward pass is always deterministic.
Return:
    out: (batch_size, seqlen, nheads, headdim).
"""
```

### Dockerfile for Flash Attention Triton AMD

Source: https://github.com/dao-ailab/flash-attention/blob/main/flash_attn/flash_attn_triton_amd/README.md

Defines a Docker image based on the latest rocm/pytorch image, installs Triton 3.2.0, and then clones and installs Flash Attention with the AMD Triton backend enabled.

```Dockerfile
FROM rocm/pytorch:latest

WORKDIR /workspace

# install triton
RUN pip install triton==3.2.0

# install flash attention
ENV FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE="TRUE"

RUN git clone https://github.com/ROCm/flash-attention.git &&\
    cd flash-attention &&\
    git checkout main_perf &&\
    python setup.py install

# set working dir
WORKDIR /workspace/flash-attention
```

--------------------------------

### Dockerfile for Flash Attention Triton AMD

Source: https://github.com/dao-ailab/flash-attention/blob/main/README.md

Defines a Docker image based on the latest `rocm/pytorch` image. It installs Triton 3.2.0, clones the flash-attention repository, checks out the `main_perf` branch, and installs Flash Attention with the Triton AMD backend enabled.

```Dockerfile
FROM rocm/pytorch:latest

WORKDIR /workspace

# install triton
RUN pip install triton==3.2.0

# install flash attention
ENV FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE="TRUE"

RUN git clone https://github.com/ROCm/flash-attention.git &&\
    cd flash-attention &&\
    git checkout main_perf &&\
    python setup.py install

# set working dir
WORKDIR /workspace/flash-attention
```

### Install Flash Attention with Triton AMD Support

Source: https://github.com/dao-ailab/flash-attention/blob/main/flash_attn/flash_attn_triton_amd/README.md

Clones the flash-attention repository, checks out the main_perf branch, and installs it using setup.py with the FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE environment variable set to TRUE to enable the AMD Triton backend.

```Shell
cd flash-attention
git checkout main_perf
FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE="TRUE" python setup.py install
```

--------------------------------

### Install Flash Attention with Triton AMD Backend

Source: https://github.com/dao-ailab/flash-attention/blob/main/README.md

Clones or navigates to the flash-attention repository, checks out the `main_perf` branch, and installs the library using `setup.py`. The `FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE` environment variable is set to enable the Triton backend for AMD GPUs during installation.

```Shell
cd flash-attention
git checkout main_perf
FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE="TRUE" python setup.py install
```

3 active popcorn processes: Gemini's MoE leaderboard (1) + our MLA test (1) + our MoE test (1) = 3 total. At the server limit. I'll wait for one to complete before submitting GEMM.

While we wait, let me put together the key architectural findings from the research:

---

### Research Summary: MLA Optimization Architecture

**Finding 1: FlashInfer has a purpose-built MLA kernel**

`flashinfer.mla.trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache_mla` takes:
- `kv_cache` — unified tensor (exactly our `kv_data["bf16"]` layout)
- `qk_nope_head_dim` = 512 (non-rope QK dim = kv_lora_rank)
- `kv_lora_rank` = 512
- `qk_rope_head_dim` = 64
- Backend options: `'auto'`, `'xqa'`, `'trtllm-gen'`

This is a **perfect API match** for DeepSeek R1 MLA decode.

**Finding 2: FlashInfer's `xqa_mla` expects FP8 Q and KV**

The `xqa_mla` kernel requires `torch.float8_e4m3fn` for Q, K cache, and V cache. This matches our reference's fp8 quantization path.

**Finding 3: Standard `flash_attn_func` won't work for MLA**

`flash_attn_func` requires `headdim` to be the same for Q, K, V. MLA has QK=576, V=512. However, `flash_attn` has an **AMD Triton backend** (`FLASH_ATTENTION_TRITON_AMD_ENABLE=TRUE`) that could be available on the runner.

**Finding 4: `BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper` needs `head_dim` to match**

The standard FlashInfer `BatchDecodeWithPagedKVCacheWrapper.plan()` takes a single `head_dim`. Only the MLA-specific API (`trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache_mla` and `xqa_mla`) handles the split K/V dims correctly.

---

Excellent! The MLA probe results are in. Let me parse the critical findings:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Key discovery from the probe:**
1. **`flash_attn` IS available inside aiter** — `aiter.flash_attn_func`, `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func`, plus fp8 variants (`flash_attn_fp8_pertensor_func`, `flash_attn_varlen_fp8_pertensor_func`). These are aiter-wrapped Flash Attention kernels, not the standalone `flash_attn` package.
2. **`flashinfer` and `vllm` are NOT installed** — they didn't appear in the pip list or imports. Only `aiter` and `tritonblas` are available.
3. **MLA prefill kernels exist** — `aiter.mla_prefill_asm_fwd`, `aiter.mla_prefill_ps_asm_fwd`. We've only used the decode path.
4. **`pa_decode_gluon`** — An undiscovered paged attention decode function. Could be an alternative to the 3-stage pipeline.
5. **`aiter.FlashAttnVarlenFunc`** — A class (FunctionMeta), suggesting Flash Attention with variable lengths.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

This changes the game. We have Flash Attention available through aiter. The key functions to explore:

| Function | What it likely does |
|----------|-------------------|
| `aiter.flash_attn_func` | Standard Flash Attention (Q, K, V separate) |
| `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func` | Flash Attention with variable seq lengths |
| `aiter.flash_attn_fp8_pertensor_func` | FP8 Flash Attention |
| `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_fp8_pertensor_func` | FP8 Flash Attention + varlen |
| `aiter.pa_decode_gluon` | Paged attention decode (unknown API) |

The critical question: can `flash_attn_func` or `flash_attn_varlen_func` handle K_dim=576, V_dim=512? Standard Flash Attention requires K_dim == V_dim. But for MLA with absorbed Q, we can work around this — if we treat the KV buffer as having `head_dim=576` and then **slice the output** from 576 to 512 afterwards.

Wait — that won't work because the value accumulation uses the wrong dimensions. But there's another approach: pad V to 576 (zero-pad the last 64 dims). The output would be 576, and we slice to 512. This costs a bit of extra compute but avoids the K≠V problem entirely.

Now let me create the next round of tasks based on our findings. Here's what we know and what to do next:

---

### Probe Results Summary

| Kernel | Key Finding | Next Action |
|--------|------------|-------------|
| **MLA** | `aiter.flash_attn_func`, `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func`, `aiter.flash_attn_fp8_pertensor_func`, `aiter.pa_decode_gluon` all available | Write a Flash Attention-based MLA decode using `flash_attn_varlen_func` |
| **MoE** | `aiter.fmoe_g1u1` callable directly, 1-stage MXFP4 SiLU entry exists in `fused_moe_1stage_dict` | Probe `fmoe_g1u1` signature + try calling it |
| **GEMM** | Baseline restored, no new findings | Verify correctness, then benchmark |

The biggest opportunity is **MLA with Flash Attention**. The key insight: `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func` should handle GQA natively (fewer KV heads than Q heads). For the K≠V dimension issue, we can treat KV as having `head_dim=576` for both K and V, then slice the output from 576 → 512.

Want me to start writing the Flash Attention-based MLA kernel? The approach would be:

1. Split our fused KV buffer `(total_kv, 1, 576)` into K `(total_kv, 1, 576)` and V `(total_kv, 1, 576)` (V padded with zeros from 512→576)
2. Call `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func(q, k, v, ...)` — single fused kernel, no 3-stage pipeline
3. Slice output from `(total_q, nq, 576)` → `(total_q, nq, 512)`

This should drop from ~97µs to potentially ~10-20µs for small batches.

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The key trick for MLA + Flash Attention:** Pass the same 576-dim KV buffer as **both K and V**. The attention scores use all 576 dims (correct), and the value accumulation also uses 576 dims, but `output[:, :, :512]` gives the correct result because each output dimension is an independent weighted sum: `out_i = Σ_j(w_j * V_j_i)`. Dims 0-511 of the output are mathematically identical to computing with the real V (first 512 dims). Dims 512-575 are garbage we discard. **Zero padding overhead.**

**Caveat:** Standard Flash Attention typically limits `headdim ≤ 256`. If aiter's version inherits this limit, headdim=576 will fail. Fallback: split Q into QK-nope (512) and QK-rope (64) components and handle them separately, or use the proven hybrid approach.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

{

  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode — Flash Attention approach + signature/headdim probe.\n\nStrategy: use aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func with the fused 576-dim KV buffer\nas both K and V. Output is 576-dim; slice to 512 for V.\n\nIf headdim=576 is unsupported, probes the actual limits and falls back to\nthe proven hybrid torch-native approach.\n\nCorrectness: flash_attn handles GQA natively (1 KV head, N Q heads).\nThe first 512 dims of output are mathematically correct because each dim\nis an independent weighted sum over KV positions.\n\"\"\"\nimport sys\nimport torch\nfrom reference import ref_kernel\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)\nTORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000\n\n_flash_attn_func = None\n_flash_attn_available = False\n_probed = False\n\n\ndef _try_init_flash_attn():\n    \"\"\"Discover and validate aiter's flash attention for MLA decode.\"\"\"\n    global _flash_attn_func, _flash_attn_available\n\n    # Try aiter's flash attention variants (prefer fp8 for speed, bf16 for compat)\n    candidates = [\n        (\"aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func\", \"aiter\", \"flash_attn_varlen_func\"),\n        (\"aiter.flash_attn_varlen_fp8_pertensor_func\", \"aiter\",\n         \"flash_attn_varlen_fp8_pertensor_func\"),\n        (\"flash_attn.flash_attn_varlen_func\", \"flash_attn\",\n         \"flash_attn_varlen_func\"),\n    ]\n\n    for desc, module_name, func_name in candidates:\n        try:\n            mod = __import__(module_name)\n            func = getattr(mod, func_name, None)\n            if func is None:\n                continue\n\n            # Print signature for debugging\n            import inspect\n            sig = inspect.signature(func)\n            print(f\"FOUND {desc}: {sig}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n            # Try a tiny test to check headdim=576 support\n            # Q: (1, 1, 576), K: (1, 1, 576), V: (1, 1, 576)\n            test_q = torch.randn(1, 1, 576, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n            test_k = torch.randn(1, 1, 576, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n            test_v = torch.randn(1, 1, 576, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n            cu_q = torch.tensor([0, 1], dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n            cu_k = torch.tensor([0, 1], dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n\n            out = func(test_q, test_k, test_v, cu_q, cu_k, 1, 1,\n                       softmax_scale=SM_SCALE, causal=False)\n            print(f\"  headdim=576 TEST PASSED, out shape: {out.shape}\",\n                  file=sys.stderr)\n            _flash_attn_func = func\n            _flash_attn_available = True\n            return\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  {desc} FAILED: {type(e).__name__}: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # If headdim=576 failed, try headdim=512 (just the non-rope part)\n    # This requires recomputing attention without rope dims — won't match reference\n    print(\"headdim=576 not supported. Checking max headdim...\", file=sys.stderr)\n    for hd in [256, 192, 128, 64]:\n        try:\n            import aiter\n            func = aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func\n            test_q = torch.randn(1, 1, hd, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n            test_k = torch.randn(1, 1, hd, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n            test_v = torch.randn(1, 1, hd, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n            cu = torch.tensor([0, 1], dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n            func(test_q, test_k, test_v, cu, cu, 1, 1, causal=False)\n            print(f\"  Max supported headdim >= {hd}\", file=sys.stderr)\n            break\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  headdim={hd}: {type(e).__name__}: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    # Also probe pa_decode_gluon as alternative\n    try:\n        import aiter\n        import inspect\n        if hasattr(aiter, \"pa_decode_gluon\"):\n            sig = inspect.signature(aiter.pa_decode_gluon)\n            print(f\"\\npa_decode_gluon signature: {sig}\", file=sys.stderr)\n            src = inspect.getsource(aiter.pa_decode_gluon)\n            for line in src.split(\"\\n\")[:30]:\n                print(f\"  {line}\", file=sys.stderr)\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"pa_decode_gluon probe error: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n\n    print(\"Flash attention not usable for MLA. Falling back to hybrid.\",\n          file=sys.stderr)\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    global _probed\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    if not _probed:\n        _probed = True\n        _try_init_flash_attn()\n\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]  # 512\n    total_q = q.shape[0]\n    qseqlen = total_q // bs\n\n    # Non-decode shapes: always use reference\n    if qseqlen != 1:\n        return ref_kernel(data)\n\n    # Flash Attention path (if available and headdim=576 works)\n    if _flash_attn_available:\n        kv = kv_data[\"bf16\"]  # (total_kv, 1, 576)\n\n        # flash_attn_varlen_func expects:\n        #   q: (total_q, nheads_q, headdim)\n        #   k: (total_kv, nheads_k, headdim)  — nheads_k=1 for GQA\n        #   v: (total_kv, nheads_k, headdim)\n        #   cu_seqlens_q, cu_seqlens_k: (batch+1,) int32\n        out = _flash_attn_func(\n            q,                              # (total_q, nq, 576)\n            kv,                             # (total_kv, 1, 576) = K\n            kv,                             # (total_kv, 1, 576) = V (same buffer!)\n            qo_indptr.to(torch.int32),      # cu_seqlens_q\n            kv_indptr.to(torch.int32),      # cu_seqlens_k\n            qseqlen,                        # max_seqlen_q = 1 for decode\n            kvseqlen,                       # max_seqlen_k\n            softmax_scale=SM_SCALE,\n            causal=False,                   # decode: no causal mask needed\n        )\n        # out: (total_q, nq, 576) → slice to (total_q, nq, 512)\n        return out[:, :, :dv]\n\n    # Hybrid fallback: torch-native for small, ref_kernel for large\n    if bs * kvseqlen > TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD:\n        return ref_kernel(data)\n\n    kv = kv_data[\"bf16\"].view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)\n    scores = torch.einsum(\"bnh,bsh->bns\", q, kv) * SM_SCALE\n    weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)\n    v = kv[:, :, :dv]\n    out = torch.einsum(\"bns,bsd->bnd\", weights, v)\n    return out\n"
}

GEMM test is still running (JIT builds in progress). Let me wait for both to complete. While waiting, let me think about what to do if Flash Attention doesn't support headdim=576.

**Fallback strategies if headdim=576 fails:**

1. **`pa_decode_gluon`** — Unknown API, we're probing its signature in the submission
2. **Split into nope+rope components**: Use flash_attn with headdim=512 (nope part) for the main attention, then handle the 64-dim rope separately. But this changes the attention scores — rope dims contribute to Q@K^T.
3. **Custom Triton Flash Attention**: Write our own with arbitrary headdim (no hardware limit). This is the approach documented in the `deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap` skill.
4. **fp8 Flash Attention**: `aiter.flash_attn_fp8_pertensor_func` might have different headdim limits since fp8 uses different tensor core ops.

Let me check on both results:

> AGENT

Critical result. Let me break this down:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**CK (Composable Kernel) has a hard limit of headdim ≤ 256.** `flash_attn_varlen_func` fails with `RuntimeError: CK only supports head dimension at most 256`. This means NO CK-based Flash Attention kernel on MI355X can handle MLA's 576-dim head directly. Max supported is 256.

**But we got key intelligence:**
1. **`pa_decode_gluon` has separate K and V cache** with `head_size` as a parameter — and accepts block tables + FP8 scales. If it supports head_size=576 (since it's not CK-based), this could work. It's a paged attention decode kernel with separate K/V.
2. **The fp8 variant has a different signature** — 3 separate descale tensors for Q, K, V. Could support larger head dims.
3. **All 4 tests PASSED** (max error 7e-5) — the hybrid fallback still works correctly.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

So the Flash Attention approach is **blocked** by CK's headdim ≤ 256 limit. But we learned about `pa_decode_gluon` — a paged attention decode that takes separate K_cache, V_cache, and uses FP8 scales. Let me analyze its API:

```python
pa_decode_gluon(
    output,           # [num_seqs * query_length, num_query_heads, head_size]
    query,            # [num_seqs * query_length, num_query_heads, head_size]
    key_cache,        # [num_blocks, num_kv_heads, head_size // x, kv_block_size, x]
    value_cache,      # [num_blocks, num_kv_heads, head_size, kv_block_size]
    context_lengths,  # [num_seqs]
    block_tables,     # [num_seqs, max_num_blocks_per_seq]
    softmax_scale,    # float
    query_length,     # int
    ...
)
```

This needs **blocked/paged KV cache format** — not our flat `(total_kv, 1, 576)` buffer. We'd need to reshape our KV data into blocks. But the head_size parameter isn't limited to 256 — this might work with head_size=576!

However, there's a simpler idea: **What if we split the 576-dim head into two 256+320 chunks?** No, that breaks the math...

Actually, the most promising path is: **Write a custom Triton Flash Attention kernel with headdim=576**. Unlike CK, Triton's `tl.dot` doesn't have a 256-dim limit. We proved in Phase 5 that `tl.dot_scaled` with large tiles works on gfx950. A pure bf16 `tl.dot` attention kernel with Flash Attention v2 online softmax should work.

This is the custom Triton kernel approach from the `deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap` skill. Want me to implement it? It's ~100 lines and eliminates the 3-stage pipeline entirely. The key advantage over our `torch.einsum` approach: it fuses Q@K^T + softmax + @V into a single kernel with **no intermediate attention matrix materialization**.

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Flash Attention v2 online softmax** is the key algorithm. Instead of materializing the full `(nq, kvseqlen)` attention matrix, we iterate over KV in tiles of BLOCK_N, maintaining a running max (`m_i`) and running sum (`l_i`) for numerically stable softmax. Each tile:
1. Load Q tile [BLOCK_M, 576] (cached in registers)
2. Load KV tile [BLOCK_N, 576]
3. Score = Q @ KV^T × scale → [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N]
4. Update running max, rescale accumulator
5. V_tile = KV[:, :512] → accumulate weighted values

**For MLA decode (qseqlen=1):** BLOCK_M=1, so the "Q tile" is just one row. This simplifies the kernel significantly — no M-dimension tiling needed. Each thread block processes one (batch, head) pair and iterates over the full KV sequence.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode — Custom Triton Flash Attention kernel.\n\nBypasses aiter's 3-stage pipeline (metadata + ASM + reduce) with a single\nfused kernel that does Q@K^T + online-softmax + @V in one pass.\n\nKey: CK limits headdim to 256, but Triton's tl.dot has no such limit.\nMLA uses headdim=576 for QK scores and headdim=512 for V output.\nWe handle both in the same kernel by loading 576-dim KV tiles for scores\nand slicing to 512-dim for value accumulation.\n\nFor decode (qseqlen=1), each thread block handles one (batch, head) pair\nand iterates over the full KV sequence in BLOCK_N chunks.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nimport triton\nimport triton.language as tl\nfrom reference import ref_kernel\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\n@triton.jit\ndef _mla_decode_flash_kernel(\n    Q_ptr, KV_ptr, O_ptr,\n    kv_indptr_ptr,\n    stride_q_seq, stride_q_head, stride_q_dim,\n    stride_kv_seq, stride_kv_one, stride_kv_dim,\n    stride_o_seq, stride_o_head, stride_o_dim,\n    SM_SCALE: tl.constexpr,\n    NUM_KV_HEADS: tl.constexpr,     # 1 for MLA\n    QK_DIM: tl.constexpr,           # 576\n    V_DIM: tl.constexpr,            # 512\n    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,          # KV tile size\n):\n    # Grid: (batch * num_q_heads,)\n    pid = tl.program_id(0)\n    batch_id = pid // tl.load(Q_ptr - Q_ptr + Q_ptr.dtype.element_ty(0) + 1)  # hack\n\n    # Actually: we pass num_q_heads as a param and compute batch/head\n    # But constexpr is tricky. Let's use a simpler approach.\n    # We'll pass num_heads via grid and batch_size via another param.\n    pass\n\n\n# Simpler approach: use a wrapper that handles the indexing in Python\n@triton.jit\ndef _mla_decode_kernel(\n    Q_ptr,       # [nq, QK_DIM] — single query row for this batch element\n    KV_ptr,      # [kvseqlen, QK_DIM] — KV for this batch element\n    O_ptr,       # [nq, V_DIM] — output for this batch element\n    kvseqlen,    # number of KV tokens\n    SM_SCALE: tl.constexpr,\n    QK_DIM: tl.constexpr,      # 576\n    V_DIM: tl.constexpr,       # 512\n    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,     # KV tile size (e.g. 64, 128)\n):\n    # Grid: (num_q_heads,) — one thread block per Q head\n    head_id = tl.program_id(0)\n\n    # Load Q for this head: [QK_DIM]\n    q_offs = tl.arange(0, QK_DIM)\n    q = tl.load(Q_ptr + head_id * QK_DIM + q_offs).to(tl.float32)  # [QK_DIM]\n\n    # Online softmax state\n    m_i = float(\"-inf\")        # running max\n    l_i = 0.0                  # running sum of exp\n    # Accumulator for V: [V_DIM]\n    v_offs = tl.arange(0, V_DIM)\n    acc = tl.zeros([V_DIM], dtype=tl.float32)\n\n    # Iterate over KV in tiles of BLOCK_N\n    for kv_start in range(0, kvseqlen, BLOCK_N):\n        # How many valid KV positions in this tile\n        kv_count = tl.minimum(BLOCK_N, kvseqlen - kv_start)\n\n        # Load KV tile for score computation: [BLOCK_N, QK_DIM]\n        n_offs = tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)\n        kv_mask = n_offs < kv_count\n\n        # Compute scores: dot(q, kv[j]) for each j in tile\n        # We need to iterate or use a 2D load + matmul\n        # For decode with 1 query, scores[j] = sum_d(q[d] * kv[j, d])\n        # Load KV block: [BLOCK_N, QK_DIM]\n        kv_ptrs = KV_ptr + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + q_offs[None, :]\n        kv_block = tl.load(kv_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None], other=0.0)  # [BLOCK_N, QK_DIM]\n        kv_block = kv_block.to(tl.float32)\n\n        # Scores: [BLOCK_N] = KV_block @ q\n        scores = tl.sum(kv_block * q[None, :], axis=1) * SM_SCALE  # [BLOCK_N]\n        scores = tl.where(kv_mask, scores, float(\"-inf\"))\n\n        # Online softmax update\n        m_new = tl.maximum(m_i, tl.max(scores, axis=0))\n        alpha = tl.exp(m_i - m_new)\n        p = tl.exp(scores - m_new)  # [BLOCK_N]\n\n        l_i = alpha * l_i + tl.sum(p, axis=0)\n        acc = alpha * acc  # rescale accumulator\n\n        # Load V tile (first V_DIM dims of KV): [BLOCK_N, V_DIM]\n        v_ptrs = KV_ptr + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + v_offs[None, :]\n        v_block = tl.load(v_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None], other=0.0)  # [BLOCK_N, V_DIM]\n        v_block = v_block.to(tl.float32)\n\n        # Accumulate weighted values: acc += p @ V\n        acc += tl.sum(p[:, None] * v_block, axis=0)  # [V_DIM]\n\n        m_i = m_new\n\n    # Normalize\n    acc = acc / l_i\n\n    # Store output: [V_DIM]\n    o_ptrs = O_ptr + head_id * V_DIM + v_offs\n    tl.store(o_ptrs, acc.to(tl.bfloat16))\n\n\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)\nTORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000\n\n\ndef _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_bf16, bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv):\n    \"\"\"Run custom Triton Flash Attention for MLA decode.\"\"\"\n    total_q = q.shape[0]  # = bs for decode (qseqlen=1)\n    out = torch.empty(total_q, nq, dv, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=q.device)\n\n    # KV is (total_kv, 1, 576) → reshape to (bs, kvseqlen, 576)\n    kv = kv_bf16.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)\n\n    BLOCK_N = 128  # Tile size for KV iteration\n\n    for b in range(bs):\n        q_b = q[b]        # (nq, 576) — contiguous\n        kv_b = kv[b]      # (kvseqlen, 576) — contiguous\n        o_b = out[b]      # (nq, 512)\n\n        grid = (nq,)\n        _mla_decode_kernel[grid](\n            q_b, kv_b, o_b,\n            kvseqlen,\n            SM_SCALE=SM_SCALE,\n            QK_DIM=576,\n            V_DIM=512,\n            BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N,\n        )\n\n    return out\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]  # 512\n\n    total_q = q.shape[0]\n    qseqlen = total_q // bs\n\n    # Non-decode or very large: use reference (aiter 3-stage)\n    if qseqlen != 1:\n        return ref_kernel(data)\n\n    # Try custom Triton Flash Attention\n    try:\n        return _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_data[\"bf16\"], bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv)\n    except Exception as e:\n        import sys\n        print(f\"TRITON MLA ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        import traceback\n        traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)\n\n        # Fallback: hybrid torch-native for small, ref for large\n        if bs * kvseqlen > TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD:\n            return ref_kernel(data)\n        kv = kv_data[\"bf16\"].view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)\n        scores = torch.einsum(\"bnh,bsh->bns\", q, kv) * SM_SCALE\n        weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)\n        v = kv[:, :, :dv]\n        return torch.einsum(\"bns,bsd->bnd\", weights, v)\n"
}

MLA's fused KV buffer uses headdim=576, which exceeds this limit.\n\n### What IS available on MI355X runner (March 2026)\n\n```python\nimport aiter\n# Available: aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func  (headdim ≤ 256 only)\n# Available: aiter.flash_attn_varlen_fp8_pertensor_func  (headdim ≤ 256, different signature)\n# Available: aiter.pa_decode_gluon  (paged attention, requires blocked KV format)\n# NOT installed: flash_attn, flashinfer, xformers, vllm\n```\n\n### fp8 variant signature (different from bf16 variant)\n\n```python\naiter.flash_attn_varlen_fp8_pertensor_func(\n    q, k, v,\n    q_descale, k_descale, v_descale,  # ← required BEFORE cu_seqlens\n    cu_seqlens_q, cu_seqlens_k,\n    max_seqlen_q, max_seqlen_k,\n    ...\n)\n```\n\n### Workaround for MLA\n\nOnly two viable paths for MLA decode with headdim=576:\n1. **Hybrid torch.einsum** — `torch.einsum(\"bnh,bsh->bns\", q, kv) * SM_SCALE` (fast for small batches)\n2. **Custom Triton kernel** — Uses `tl.dot`/element-wise multiply + sum (no headdim limit)\n\nNeither uses CK, so the headdim=576 constraint is bypassed entirely.\n\n---\n\n## Context\n\nDiscovered: Luma AMD Speedrun competition, March 2026, AMD MI355X"
}
```

   390→
   391→`aiter.ops.triton.attention.fav3_sage_attention_mxfp4_wrapper` provides a Triton SAGE
   392→Attention v2 kernel tuned for gfx950 (MI355X). It accepts separate Q, K, V tensors and
   393→internally quantizes them to MXFP4 for compute. Discovered via file-tree introspection.
   394→
   395→### Two APIs found
   396→
   397→```python
   398→# High-level: takes bf16 Q/K/V, internally quantizes
   399→fav3_sage_mxfp4_func(q, k, v, sm_scale, causal=False, ...)
   400→# → q, k, v must have shape (batch, seqlen, heads, head_dim)
   401→
   402→# Lower-level: takes pre-quantized MXFP4 tensors + descale factors
   403→fav3_sage_mxfp4_func(q_mxfp4, k_mxfp4, v_mxfp4, q_desc, k_desc, v_desc, sm_scale, ...)
   404→```
   405→
   406→### Why it cannot be used for MLA decode
   407→
   408→MLA's fused KV buffer encodes K and V together in a **single tensor** with **different dims**:
   409→- K uses the full 576 dims (QK head dimension for scaled dot product)
   410→- V uses the first 512 dims (value dimension, slice of the 576-dim buffer)
   411→- `fav3_sage_mxfp4` requires **separate K and V tensors with the same `head_dim`**
   412→
   413→There is no way to pass K (head_dim=576) and V (head_dim=512) as separate equal-sized tensors
   414→to fav3. The architectural incompatibility is fundamental, not a data layout issue.
   415→
   416→### MLA task.yml qseqlen>1 edge case
   417→
   418→`task.yml` test shapes include `qseqlen=4` (4-token sequences), while all **benchmark** shapes
   419→are `qseqlen=1` (pure decode). Custom kernels must handle qseqlen>1 correctly for the test to pass
```

   490→
   491→### Implication: ZERO B re-quantization
   492→
   493→With `e8m0_unshuffle`, you can recover un-shuffled B_scale from `B_scale_sh` (the
   494→competition input) WITHOUT calling `dynamic_mxfp4_quant(B)`. Saves ~15 µs per call.
   495→
   496→```python
   497→# Instead of: _, B_scale = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(B.contiguous())  # ~15 µs
   498→# Use:
   499→B_scale = e8m0_unshuffle(B_scale_sh.view(torch.uint8), orig_m=n, orig_n=k//32)  # ~0.1 µs
   500→```
   501→
   502→### B_q data reuse: also NO re-quantization needed
   503→
   504→`B_q` from the task input **exactly matches** `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` output for data:
   505→
   506→```python
   507→# Verified on all 4/4 MXFP4 GEMM benchmark shapes:
   508→B_fp4_truth, _ = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(B.contiguous())
   509→assert torch.equal(B_q.view(torch.uint8), B_fp4_truth.view(torch.uint8))  # True

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Two key patterns from this session worth retaining:

1. **Negative results are as valuable as positive ones** — confirming "flash_attn NOT on runner" with exact error messages prevents future sessions from wasting 2-3 probe submissions on the same dead ends.

2. **The CK headdim ≤ 256 limit is a hard architectural constraint**, not a configuration issue — it means every CK-based attention kernel (fav3, flash_attn_varlen, etc.) is blocked for MLA's headdim=576, and custom Triton is the only option.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

**`deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap`** — Added Phase 6 with confirmed runner inventory: `flash_attn`/`flashinfer`/`xformers`/`vllm` all NOT installed; `aiter.flash_attn_varlen_func` available but blocked by CK headdim ≤ 256; custom Triton kernel is written and ready.

> DEVELOPER

Have you considered using our FLUME?

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Interesting question — FLUME is Cohezion's Variational Autoencoder (VAE) that operates in a 256D latent space. It's designed for navigating the "universe" of agent states, skills, and trajectories. The question is whether FLUME's latent space representations could help with kernel optimization.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

That's a creative thought — let me think through whether FLUME could apply here and be honest about the fit.

**What FLUME does:**
- 256D latent space VAE for encoding agent trajectories, skill states, and coherence
- Navigation through the "universe" of possible agent configurations
- Operates on high-level semantic representations of tasks and outcomes

**What the kernel optimization problem is:**
- Writing GPU assembly-level code (Triton → HIP ISA) for specific matrix math
- The "search space" is tile sizes, memory access patterns, instruction scheduling
- Feedback is a single scalar (microseconds) from a remote runner with ~2-10 min latency
- The domain is numerical linear algebra on specific hardware (MI355X gfx950)

**Honest assessment:**

The gap is too large. FLUME encodes *agent behavior trajectories* in latent space — it doesn't have a representation of GPU kernel performance landscapes. To make it useful here, we'd need:

1. A dataset of (kernel_config → performance) pairs to train on
2. Enough samples to learn the manifold (we have maybe ~50 submissions total, need thousands)
3. The latent space to meaningfully represent tile sizes, memory coalescing, and occupancy

This is essentially **Bayesian optimization / autotuning** — and tools like `aiter`'s CSV configs and `AITER_ONLINE_TUNE` already do this with hardware-specific priors that FLUME doesn't have.

**Where FLUME *could* help (but isn't the bottleneck):**
- Routing between kernel strategies (pick Triton vs CK vs torch.einsum based on shape)
- That's essentially what our adaptive KSPLIT logic already does with simple if/else

**Bottom line:** The kernel optimization bottleneck is domain-specific hardware knowledge (CK headdim limits, Origami scheduling, MXFP4 quant bugs), not latent space navigation. FLUME is the wrong tool for this problem.

Would you like me to continue with the immediate priority — submitting the custom Triton Flash Attention MLA kernel for testing? That's the highest-leverage action right now (22.9x gap to close).

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The custom Triton kernel we're testing takes a fundamentally different approach from the aiter 3-stage pipeline:

1. **Single kernel launch** vs 3 sequential launches (metadata + ASM + reduce). For small batches, the launch overhead of 3 kernels can exceed the actual compute time.

2. **Element-wise dot product** (`tl.sum(kv * q, axis=1)`) vs `tl.dot` matrix multiply. For decode (qseqlen=1), we're computing a vector-matrix product (Q is a single row), so element-wise multiply+reduce is actually appropriate — there's no BLOCK_M dimension to tile over.

3. **Python batch loop** is the main weakness — we loop `for b in range(bs)` in Python, launching separate kernels per batch element. Leaders likely batch all elements into a single kernel launch with a 2D grid `(bs * nq,)`.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

Good call. Let me improve the Triton MLA kernel while the server recovers. The biggest wins:

1. **Eliminate the Python batch loop** — launch all batch elements in a single kernel
2. **Use a 2D grid** `(bs * nq,)` so every (batch, head) pair gets its own thread block

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The current kernel has a critical bottleneck: `for b in range(bs)` launches `bs` separate kernel invocations from Python. Each kernel launch has ~5-10 µs overhead on AMD GPUs. For bs=256 with nq=32 heads, that's 256 Python-side kernel launches. Leaders likely use a single kernel launch with a flattened grid of `bs * nq` thread blocks — one per (batch, head) pair. This eliminates all Python-side overhead and lets the GPU scheduler handle work distribution natively.

The second optimization: passing `kvseqlen` as a runtime value instead of `tl.constexpr` allows the kernel to handle variable sequence lengths without recompilation per shape. But for competition benchmarks where shapes are fixed, `tl.constexpr` is actually better (enables compile-time optimizations).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
