```
     1→"""
     2→MLA decode — Custom Triton Flash Attention kernel.
     3→
     4→Bypasses aiter's 3-stage pipeline (metadata + ASM + reduce) with a single
     5→fused kernel that does Q@K^T + online-softmax + @V in one pass.
     6→
     7→Key: CK limits headdim to 256, but Triton's tl.dot has no such limit.
     8→MLA uses headdim=576 for QK scores and headdim=512 for V output.
     9→We handle both in the same kernel by loading 576-dim KV tiles for scores
    10→and slicing to 512-dim for value accumulation.
    11→
    12→For decode (qseqlen=1), each thread block handles one (batch, head) pair
    13→and iterates over the full KV sequence in BLOCK_N chunks.
    14→"""
    15→import torch
    16→import triton
    17→import triton.language as tl
    18→from reference import ref_kernel
    19→from task import input_t, output_t
    20→
    21→
    22→@triton.jit
    23→def _mla_decode_flash_kernel(
    24→    Q_ptr, KV_ptr, O_ptr,
    25→    kv_indptr_ptr,
    26→    stride_q_seq, stride_q_head, stride_q_dim,
    27→    stride_kv_seq, stride_kv_one, stride_kv_dim,
    28→    stride_o_seq, stride_o_head, stride_o_dim,
    29→    SM_SCALE: tl.constexpr,
    30→    NUM_KV_HEADS: tl.constexpr,     # 1 for MLA
    31→    QK_DIM: tl.constexpr,           # 576
    32→    V_DIM: tl.constexpr,            # 512
    33→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,          # KV tile size
    34→):
    35→    # Grid: (batch * num_q_heads,)
    36→    pid = tl.program_id(0)
    37→    batch_id = pid // tl.load(Q_ptr - Q_ptr + Q_ptr.dtype.element_ty(0) + 1)  # hack
    38→
    39→    # Actually: we pass num_q_heads as a param and compute batch/head
    40→    # But constexpr is tricky. Let's use a simpler approach.
    41→    # We'll pass num_heads via grid and batch_size via another param.
    42→    pass
    43→
    44→
    45→# Simpler approach: use a wrapper that handles the indexing in Python
    46→@triton.jit
    47→def _mla_decode_kernel(
    48→    Q_ptr,       # [nq, QK_DIM] — single query row for this batch element
    49→    KV_ptr,      # [kvseqlen, QK_DIM] — KV for this batch element
    50→    O_ptr,       # [nq, V_DIM] — output for this batch element
    51→    kvseqlen,    # number of KV tokens
    52→    SM_SCALE: tl.constexpr,
    53→    QK_DIM: tl.constexpr,      # 576
    54→    V_DIM: tl.constexpr,       # 512
    55→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,     # KV tile size (e.g. 64, 128)
    56→):
    57→    # Grid: (num_q_heads,) — one thread block per Q head
    58→    head_id = tl.program_id(0)
    59→
    60→    # Load Q for this head: [QK_DIM]
    61→    q_offs = tl.arange(0, QK_DIM)
    62→    q = tl.load(Q_ptr + head_id * QK_DIM + q_offs).to(tl.float32)  # [QK_DIM]
    63→
    64→    # Online softmax state
    65→    m_i = float("-inf")        # running max
    66→    l_i = 0.0                  # running sum of exp
    67→    # Accumulator for V: [V_DIM]
    68→    v_offs = tl.arange(0, V_DIM)
    69→    acc = tl.zeros([V_DIM], dtype=tl.float32)
    70→
    71→    # Iterate over KV in tiles of BLOCK_N
    72→    for kv_start in range(0, kvseqlen, BLOCK_N):
    73→        # How many valid KV positions in this tile
    74→        kv_count = tl.minimum(BLOCK_N, kvseqlen - kv_start)
    75→
    76→        # Load KV tile for score computation: [BLOCK_N, QK_DIM]
    77→        n_offs = tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
    78→        kv_mask = n_offs < kv_count
    79→
    80→        # Compute scores: dot(q, kv[j]) for each j in tile
    81→        # We need to iterate or use a 2D load + matmul
    82→        # For decode with 1 query, scores[j] = sum_d(q[d] * kv[j, d])
    83→        # Load KV block: [BLOCK_N, QK_DIM]
    84→        kv_ptrs = KV_ptr + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + q_offs[None, :]
    85→        kv_block = tl.load(kv_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None], other=0.0)  # [BLOCK_N, QK_DIM]
    86→        kv_block = kv_block.to(tl.float32)
    87→
    88→        # Scores: [BLOCK_N] = KV_block @ q
    89→        scores = tl.sum(kv_block * q[None, :], axis=1) * SM_SCALE  # [BLOCK_N]
    90→        scores = tl.where(kv_mask, scores, float("-inf"))
    91→
    92→        # Online softmax update
    93→        m_new = tl.maximum(m_i, tl.max(scores, axis=0))
    94→        alpha = tl.exp(m_i - m_new)
    95→        p = tl.exp(scores - m_new)  # [BLOCK_N]
    96→
    97→        l_i = alpha * l_i + tl.sum(p, axis=0)
    98→        acc = alpha * acc  # rescale accumulator
    99→
   100→        # Load V tile (first V_DIM dims of KV): [BLOCK_N, V_DIM]
   101→        v_ptrs = KV_ptr + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + v_offs[None, :]
   102→        v_block = tl.load(v_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None], other=0.0)  # [BLOCK_N, V_DIM]
   103→        v_block = v_block.to(tl.float32)
   104→
   105→        # Accumulate weighted values: acc += p @ V
   106→        acc += tl.sum(p[:, None] * v_block, axis=0)  # [V_DIM]
   107→
   108→        m_i = m_new
   109→
   110→    # Normalize
   111→    acc = acc / l_i
   112→
   113→    # Store output: [V_DIM]
   114→    o_ptrs = O_ptr + head_id * V_DIM + v_offs
   115→    tl.store(o_ptrs, acc.to(tl.bfloat16))
   116→
   117→
   118→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)
   119→TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000
   120→
   121→
   122→def _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_bf16, bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv):
   123→    """Run custom Triton Flash Attention for MLA decode."""
   124→    total_q = q.shape[0]  # = bs for decode (qseqlen=1)
   125→    out = torch.empty(total_q, nq, dv, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=q.device)
   126→
   127→    # KV is (total_kv, 1, 576) → reshape to (bs, kvseqlen, 576)
   128→    kv = kv_bf16.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)
   129→
   130→    BLOCK_N = 128  # Tile size for KV iteration
   131→
   132→    for b in range(bs):
   133→        q_b = q[b]        # (nq, 576) — contiguous
   134→        kv_b = kv[b]      # (kvseqlen, 576) — contiguous
   135→        o_b = out[b]      # (nq, 512)
   136→
   137→        grid = (nq,)
   138→        _mla_decode_kernel[grid](
   139→            q_b, kv_b, o_b,
   140→            kvseqlen,
   141→            SM_SCALE=SM_SCALE,
   142→            QK_DIM=576,
   143→            V_DIM=512,
   144→            BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N,
   145→        )
   146→
   147→    return out
   148→
   149→
   150→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   151→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   152→
   153→    bs = config["batch_size"]
   154→    nq = config["num_heads"]
   155→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
   156→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]  # 512
   157→
   158→    total_q = q.shape[0]
   159→    qseqlen = total_q // bs
   160→
   161→    # Non-decode or very large: use reference (aiter 3-stage)
   162→    if qseqlen != 1:
   163→        return ref_kernel(data)
   164→
   165→    # Try custom Triton Flash Attention
   166→    try:
   167→        return _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_data["bf16"], bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv)
   168→    except Exception as e:
   169→        import sys
   170→        print(f"TRITON MLA ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   171→        import traceback
   172→        traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)
   173→
   174→        # Fallback: hybrid torch-native for small, ref for large
   175→        if bs * kvseqlen > TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD:
   176→            return ref_kernel(data)
   177→        kv = kv_data["bf16"].view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)
   178→        scores = torch.einsum("bnh,bsh->bns", q, kv) * SM_SCALE
   179→        weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   180→        v = kv[:, :, :dv]
   181→        return torch.einsum("bns,bsd->bnd", weights, v)
   182→
```

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode — Custom Triton Flash Attention kernel (v2: batched grid).\n\nBypasses aiter's 3-stage pipeline (metadata + ASM + reduce) with a single\nfused kernel that does Q@K^T + online-softmax + @V in one pass.\n\nKey improvements over v1:\n  - Single kernel launch for all (batch, head) pairs via flattened 1D grid\n  - No Python batch loop — GPU scheduler handles work distribution\n  - Contiguous KV view avoids per-batch pointer arithmetic in Python\n\nCK limits headdim to 256, but Triton has no such limit.\nMLA uses headdim=576 for QK scores and headdim=512 for V output.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nimport triton\nimport triton.language as tl\nfrom reference import ref_kernel\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\n@triton.jit\ndef _mla_decode_kernel(\n    Q_ptr,       # [bs, nq, QK_DIM] flattened\n    KV_ptr,      # [bs, kvseqlen, QK_DIM] flattened\n    O_ptr,       # [bs, nq, V_DIM] flattened\n    kvseqlen,    # number of KV tokens per batch element\n    NQ: tl.constexpr,             # num query heads\n    SM_SCALE: tl.constexpr,\n    QK_DIM: tl.constexpr,         # 576\n    V_DIM: tl.constexpr,          # 512\n    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,        # KV tile size (e.g. 64, 128)\n):\n    # Grid: (bs * nq,) — one thread block per (batch, head) pair\n    pid = tl.program_id(0)\n    batch_id = pid // NQ\n    head_id = pid % NQ\n\n    # Pointers for this (batch, head)\n    q_base = Q_ptr + batch_id * NQ * QK_DIM + head_id * QK_DIM\n    kv_base = KV_ptr + batch_id * kvseqlen * QK_DIM\n    o_base = O_ptr + batch_id * NQ * V_DIM + head_id * V_DIM\n\n    # Load Q for this head: [QK_DIM]\n    q_offs = tl.arange(0, QK_DIM)\n    q = tl.load(q_base + q_offs).to(tl.float32)\n\n    # Online softmax state\n    m_i = float(\"-inf\")\n    l_i = 0.0\n    v_offs = tl.arange(0, V_DIM)\n    acc = tl.zeros([V_DIM], dtype=tl.float32)\n\n    # Iterate over KV in tiles of BLOCK_N\n    for kv_start in range(0, kvseqlen, BLOCK_N):\n        kv_count = tl.minimum(BLOCK_N, kvseqlen - kv_start)\n        n_offs = tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)\n        kv_mask = n_offs < kv_count\n\n        # Load KV block: [BLOCK_N, QK_DIM]\n        kv_ptrs = kv_base + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + q_offs[None, :]\n        kv_block = tl.load(kv_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None], other=0.0).to(tl.float32)\n\n        # Scores: [BLOCK_N] = sum(KV * Q, dim=-1) * scale\n        scores = tl.sum(kv_block * q[None, :], axis=1) * SM_SCALE\n        scores = tl.where(kv_mask, scores, float(\"-inf\"))\n\n        # Online softmax update\n        m_new = tl.maximum(m_i, tl.max(scores, axis=0))\n        alpha = tl.exp(m_i - m_new)\n        p = tl.exp(scores - m_new)\n\n        l_i = alpha * l_i + tl.sum(p, axis=0)\n        acc = alpha * acc\n\n        # Load V tile (first V_DIM dims of KV): [BLOCK_N, V_DIM]\n        v_ptrs = kv_base + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + v_offs[None, :]\n        v_block = tl.load(v_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None], other=0.0).to(tl.float32)\n\n        # Accumulate weighted values\n        acc += tl.sum(p[:, None] * v_block, axis=0)\n        m_i = m_new\n\n    # Normalize and store\n    acc = acc / l_i\n    tl.store(o_base + v_offs, acc.to(tl.bfloat16))\n\n\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)\nTORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000\n\n\ndef _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_bf16, bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv):\n    \"\"\"Run custom Triton Flash Attention for MLA decode — single kernel launch.\"\"\"\n    out = torch.empty(bs, nq, dv, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=q.device)\n\n    # Reshape Q: (bs, nq, 576) contiguous\n    q_flat = q.view(bs, nq, 576).contiguous()\n    # KV: (total_kv, 1, 576) → (bs, kvseqlen, 576) contiguous\n    kv = kv_bf16.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576).contiguous()\n\n    BLOCK_N = 128\n    grid = (bs * nq,)\n    _mla_decode_kernel[grid](\n        q_flat, kv, out,\n        kvseqlen,\n        NQ=nq,\n        SM_SCALE=SM_SCALE,\n        QK_DIM=576,\n        V_DIM=512,\n        BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N,\n    )\n\n    return out\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]  # 512\n\n    total_q = q.shape[0]\n    qseqlen = total_q // bs\n\n    # Non-decode: use reference (aiter 3-stage)\n    if qseqlen != 1:\n        return ref_kernel(data)\n\n    # Try custom Triton Flash Attention\n    try:\n        return _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_data[\"bf16\"], bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv)\n    except Exception as e:\n        import sys\n        print(f\"TRITON MLA ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        import traceback\n        traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)\n\n        # > AGENT
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Key changes in v2:

1. **Flattened grid `(bs * nq,)`** — Each thread block computes `batch_id = pid // NQ` and `head_id = pid % NQ` to find its work. This replaces `bs` separate Python-side kernel launches with a single launch. For bs=256, nq=32, that's 8192 thread blocks launched at once instead of 256 sequential launches of 32 blocks each.

2. **Pointer arithmetic moved into kernel** — `q_base`, `kv_base`, `o_base` are computed from `batch_id` and `head_id` using stride math. The old version passed pre-sliced tensors `q[b]`, `kv[b]`, `out[b]` from Python.

3. **Removed dead code** — The `_mla_decode_flash_kernel` stub (lines 22-42) was a failed first attempt that was never completed. Clean code = less confusion.

4. **`.contiguous()` calls** — Critical for correctness. The `q.view(bs, nq, 576)` reshape may produce a non-contiguous tensor if the original layout differs. The kernel assumes contiguous memory (stride = dim_size), so we must ensure contiguity.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

The Triton kernel failed with `arange's range must be a power of 2` for QK_DIM=576. This is a fundamental Triton constraint I need to work around.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Critical Triton constraint:** `tl.arange(0, N)` requires N to be a power of 2. MLA uses QK_DIM=576 and V_DIM=512. While 512 is a power of 2, **576 is not** (576 = 512 + 64 = 9 × 64). This means we can't create a single `tl.arange(0, 576)` vector.

**Solution:** Pad to the next power of 2 (1024) and mask out invalid elements. Load 1024 elements from Q and KV, but mask positions 576-1023 to zero so they don't affect the dot product. This wastes some register space but is the standard Triton pattern for non-power-of-2 dimensions.

Alternatively: since V_DIM=512 IS a power of 2, we could split the score computation differently — but the simplest fix is padding.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode — Custom Triton Flash Attention kernel (v3: power-of-2 fix).\n\nBypasses aiter's 3-stage pipeline (metadata + ASM + reduce) with a single\nfused kernel that does Q@K^T + online-softmax + @V in one pass.\n\nKey: tl.arange requires power-of-2 ranges. QK_DIM=576 is NOT power-of-2,\nso we pad to QK_PAD=1024 and mask out invalid positions. V_DIM=512 is\nalready power-of-2.\n\nSingle kernel launch for all (batch, head) pairs via flattened grid.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nimport triton\nimport triton.language as tl\nfrom reference import ref_kernel\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\n@triton.jit\ndef _mla_decode_kernel(\n    Q_ptr,       # [bs, nq, QK_DIM] flattened, contiguous\n    KV_ptr,      # [bs, kvseqlen, QK_DIM] flattened, contiguous\n    O_ptr,       # [bs, nq, V_DIM] flattened, contiguous\n    kvseqlen,    # number of KV tokens per batch element\n    NQ: tl.constexpr,             # num query heads\n    SM_SCALE: tl.constexpr,\n    QK_DIM: tl.constexpr,         # 576 (actual dimension)\n    QK_PAD: tl.constexpr,         # 1024 (next power of 2 for arange)\n    V_DIM: tl.constexpr,          # 512 (already power of 2)\n    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,        # KV tile size\n):\n    pid = tl.program_id(0)\n    batch_id = pid // NQ\n    head_id = pid % NQ\n\n    q_base = Q_ptr + batch_id * NQ * QK_DIM + head_id * QK_DIM\n    kv_base = KV_ptr + batch_id * kvseqlen * QK_DIM\n    o_base = O_ptr + batch_id * NQ * V_DIM + head_id * V_DIM\n\n    # Load Q: pad to QK_PAD, mask out [QK_DIM, QK_PAD)\n    q_offs = tl.arange(0, QK_PAD)\n    q_mask = q_offs < QK_DIM\n    q = tl.load(q_base + q_offs, mask=q_mask, other=0.0).to(tl.float32)\n\n    # Online softmax state\n    m_i = float(\"-inf\")\n    l_i = 0.0\n    v_offs = tl.arange(0, V_DIM)\n    acc = tl.zeros([V_DIM], dtype=tl.float32)\n\n    for kv_start in range(0, kvseqlen, BLOCK_N):\n        kv_count = tl.minimum(BLOCK_N, kvseqlen - kv_start)\n        n_offs = tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)\n        kv_mask = n_offs < kv_count\n\n        # Load KV block for scores: [BLOCK_N, QK_PAD], masked to QK_DIM\n        kv_ptrs = kv_base + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + q_offs[None, :]\n        kv_block = tl.load(\n            kv_ptrs,\n            mask=kv_mask[:, None] & q_mask[None, :],\n            other=0.0,\n        ).to(tl.float32)\n\n        # Scores: [BLOCK_N] — masked dims contribute 0 (correct)\n        scores = tl.sum(kv_block * q[None, :], axis=1) * SM_SCALE\n        scores = tl.where(kv_mask, scores, float(\"-inf\"))\n\n        # Online softmax update\n        m_new = tl.maximum(m_i, tl.max(scores, axis=0))\n        alpha = tl.exp(m_i - m_new)\n        p = tl.exp(scores - m_new)\n\n        l_i = alpha * l_i + tl.sum(p, axis=0)\n        acc = alpha * acc\n\n        # Load V tile: [BLOCK_N, V_DIM] (first 512 dims of KV row)\n        v_ptrs = kv_base + (kv_start + n_offs[:, None]) * QK_DIM + v_offs[None, :]\n        v_block = tl.load(v_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None], other=0.0).to(tl.float32)\n\n        acc += tl.sum(p[:, None] * v_block, axis=0)\n        m_i = m_new\n\n    acc = acc / l_i\n    tl.store(o_base + v_offs, acc.to(tl.bfloat16))\n\n\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)\nTORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD = 400_000\n\n\ndef _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_bf16, bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv):\n    \"\"\"Run custom Triton Flash Attention for MLA decode — single kernel launch.\"\"\"\n    out = torch.empty(bs, nq, dv, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=q.device)\n\n    q_flat = q.view(bs, nq, 576).contiguous()\n    kv = kv_bf16.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576).contiguous()\n\n    BLOCK_N = 128\n    grid = (bs * nq,)\n    _mla_decode_kernel[grid](\n        q_flat, kv, out,\n        kvseqlen,\n        NQ=nq,\n        SM_SCALE=SM_SCALE,\n        QK_DIM=576,\n        QK_PAD=1024,\n        V_DIM=512,\n        BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N,\n    )\n\n    return out\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]  # 512\n\n    total_q = q.shape[0]\n    qseqlen = total_q // bs\n\n    if qseqlen != 1:\n        return ref_kernel(data)\n\n    try:\n        return _triton_mla_decode(q, kv_data[\"bf16\"], bs, nq, kvseqlen, dv)\n    except Exception as e:\n        import sys\n        print(f\"TRITON MLA ERROR: {type(e).__name__}: {e}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        import traceback\n        traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)\n\n        if bs * kvseqlen > TORCH_NATIVE_THRESHOLD:\n            return ref_kernel(data)\n        kv = kv_data[\"bf16\"].view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)\n        scores = torch.einsum(\"bnh,bsh->bns\", q, kv) * SM_SCALE\n        weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)\n        v = kv[:, :, :dv]\n        return torch.einsum(\"bns,bsd->bnd\", weights, v)\n"
}
```

> AGENT

**Passed 4/4 tests with excellent accuracy!** Max errors: 2.7e-5 to 7.1e-5 (well under the 1e-2 tolerance).

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The power-of-2 padding trick works perfectly. By using `QK_PAD=1024` with `tl.arange(0, 1024)` and masking positions ≥ 576 to zero, the padded elements contribute nothing to the dot product (`0 * anything = 0`). The accuracy is actually **better** than the previous torch.einsum approach (5e-5 vs 5e-5 — comparable) because both use bf16 KV data.

Note: the stderr shows aiter JIT building MLA modules (~220s total). This happens because the fallback `ref_kernel` path imports aiter even though our Triton kernel succeeded. The 4/4 passes confirm the Triton kernel was used, not the fallback.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Now let's benchmark to see if we beat the ~97 µs hybrid approach.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The Triton kernel is dramatically SLOWER than both the hybrid approach and the reference.** Here's the comparison:

| Shape | Hybrid (torch.einsum) | Triton v3 | aiter ref | Factor vs hybrid |
|-------|----------------------|-----------|-----------|-----------------|
| bs=4, kv=1k | 27.7 µs | **252 µs** | ~150 µs | 9.1x slower |
| bs=4, kv=8k | 40.5 µs | **1748 µs** | ~169 µs | 43x slower |
| bs=32, kv=1k | 46.2 µs | **712 µs** | ~179 µs | 15x slower |
| bs=32, kv=8k | 171 µs | **5400 µs** | ~206 µs | 32x slower |
| bs=256, kv=1k | 178 µs | **5520 µs** | ~196 µs | 31x slower |
| bs=256, kv=8k | 399 µs | **50500 µs** | ~397 µs | 127x slower |

**Root cause: element-wise multiply + reduce is catastrophically slow for the score computation.** Each thread block is doing `tl.sum(kv_block * q[None, :], axis=1)` — that's a [BLOCK_N, 1024] element-wise multiply followed by a horizontal sum. This is sequential register arithmetic, not using the GPU's matrix cores at all.

The leaders at 4.3 µs are almost certainly using `tl.dot` (which maps to hardware matrix multiply units) or CK assembly. Our kernel is doing SIMD arithmetic where it should be doing matrix multiply.

**The fix:** We need to restructure the score computation to use `tl.dot`. For decode (Q is a single row per head), we could reshape Q to [1, QK_DIM] and use `tl.dot(q_tile, kv_tile.T)` → [1, BLOCK_N]. But `tl.dot` requires both dimensions ≥ 16, and the QK_DIM=576 (non-power-of-2) issue complicates this further.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The Triton kernel is **much** slower than even the reference — the element-wise multiply+sum approach doesn't use matrix cores. Let me revert to the hybrid approach (our proven ~97 µs baseline) and think about this differently.

The fundamental problem: for decode, Q is a single row per head, and `tl.dot` requires minimum 16×16 tiles. The score computation `Q[1, 576] @ KV[BLOCK_N, 576]^T` maps to a vector-matrix product that doesn't efficiently map to `tl.dot`.

Let me restore the hybrid approach (proven ~97 µs geomean) which is our best-performing submission.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why the hybrid approach wins over custom Triton for MLA decode:**

1. **torch.einsum uses cuBLAS/hipBLAS** — These are vendor-optimized GEMM libraries that already use matrix cores. `torch.einsum("bnh,bsh->bns", q, kv)` maps to a batched GEMM which the hardware excels at.

2. **Decode is inherently a GEMV** (matrix-vector multiply) — Q has only 1 row per head. Matrix cores want tiles of at least 16×16. There's no way to fill a 16-row Q tile with only 1 query token. The leaders at 4.3 µs are likely: (a) using a persistent GEMV kernel tuned for MI355X's specific memory hierarchy, or (b) using CK assembly with a specialized decode path.

3. **The real bottleneck is memory bandwidth** — For bs=4, kv=1024, the KV cache is 4 × 1024 × 576 × 2 bytes = 4.7 MB. Reading this at MI355X's ~5.3 TB/s bandwidth takes ~0.9 µs. The leaders at 4.3 µs are ~4.8x above the roofline, suggesting they're compute+launch limited, not bandwidth limited. Our 27.7 µs includes Python overhead + two separate GEMM launches + softmax.

The path to 4.3 µs requires either: (a) a fused kernel in CK assembly with a specialized GEMV + softmax pipeline, or (b) a persistent Triton kernel that uses `tl.dot` by padding Q to 16 rows with zeros.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The hybrid approach is restored. This is our proven ~97 µs baseline. Let me now capture the key learning from this Triton experiment.

> AGENT

Here's a summary of what we learned:

## Results

| Approach | Geomean | vs Leader (4.3 µs) |
|----------|---------|---------------------|
| Custom Triton Flash Attn | ~3000+ µs | ~700x slower |
| Hybrid torch.einsum (restored) | ~97 µs | ~23x gap |
| aiter 3-stage reference | ~199 µs | ~46x gap |

## Key Learnings

**The Triton kernel was correct (4/4 tests passed, max error 7e-5) but catastrophically slow.** The problem is fundamental to the decode workload:

1. **Decode = GEMV** — Q has 1 row per head. `tl.dot` requires minimum 16×16 tiles. There's no way to use matrix cores for a single-row query.
2. **Element-wise multiply+sum** (`tl.sum(kv * q, axis=1)`) uses SIMD ALUs, not matrix cores — orders of magnitude slower.
3. **torch.einsum already uses hipBLAS** which is vendor-optimized for GEMV patterns.

## What Would Close the Gap

The leaders at 4.3 µs are likely using one of:
- **CK assembly** with a specialized GEMV + fused softmax kernel (not accessible from Python)
- **Persistent Triton kernel** that pads Q to 16 rows with zeros to enable `tl.dot`, fusing score+softmax+value in one pass
- **Split-K with warp-level reduction** for the KV dimension

The hybrid torch.einsum approach remains our best MLA submission at ~97 µs. Further improvement requires either CK assembly access or a fundamentally different Triton strategy (Q-padding for `tl.dot`).

Want me to explore the Q-padding approach (pad Q to 16 rows to enable `tl.dot`), or focus on the other kernels (GEMM/MoE)?

     1→---
     2→name: amd-gfx950-tl-dot-scaled-constraints
     3→description: |
     4→  Hardware constraints for Triton tl.dot_scaled with MXFP4 (e2m1) format on AMD gfx950 (MI355X).
     5→  Use when: (1) writing a Triton MXFP4 GEMM kernel using tl.dot_scaled, (2) seeing wrong results
     6→  silently with small tile sizes, (3) seeing assertion "inputVals.size() % 4 == 0" failed,
     7→  (4) debugging "lhs_scale must be tensor of shape [X, Y]" errors. Critical for correct scale
     8→  layout — RHS scale must be [BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_K] (N-first), not transposed.
     9→author: Claude Code
    10→version: 1.0.0
    11→tags: [amd, triton, gfx950, mi355x, mxfp4, tl.dot_scaled, gpu-kernels, rocm]
    12→---
    13→
    14→# AMD gfx950 `tl.dot_scaled` MXFP4 Hardware Constraints
    15→
    16→## Problem
    17→
    18→`tl.dot_scaled` with `"e2m1"` (MXFP4) format on AMD gfx950 has undocumented hardware minimums.
    19→Violating them produces **silent wrong results** (BLOCK_M too small) or a GPU assertion failure
    20→(BLOCK_K too small). The scale tensor layout for RHS is also non-obvious.
    21→
    22→## Hardware Constraints
    23→
    24→### BLOCK_M minimum: 16
    25→
    26→```python
    27→# WRONG — silently produces incorrect results on gfx950
    28→BLOCK_M = 8  # All 8 threads compute but results are wrong, no error raised
    29→
    30→# CORRECT
    31→BLOCK_M = max(16, triton.next_power_of_2(m))
    32→```
    33→
    34→**Symptom if violated:** 3/4 test shapes pass, smallest shape (m=8) fails with ~6% error.
    35→No error message — values are wrong but plausible-looking.
    36→
    37→### BLOCK_K minimum: 64 (packed uint8 bytes)
    38→
    39→```python
    40→# WRONG — GPU assertion failure
    41→BLOCK_K = 32  # Causes: "Assertion 'inputVals.size() % 4 == 0' failed"
    42→
    43→# CORRECT
    44→BLOCK_K = 64  # Minimum for ScaledUpcastFp4Op on gfx950
    45→```
    46→
    47→**Symptom if violated:** `AMDGPUKernelAssert` / `Assertion 'inputVals.size() % 4 == 0' failed`
    48→at kernel launch.
    49→
    50→## Scale Layout
    51→
    52→Each scale entry covers 32 fp4 elements = 16 packed uint8 bytes.
    53→For a K-tile of `BLOCK_K` packed bytes, you need `BLOCK_K // 16` scale entries per row.
    54→
    55→```python
    56→SCALE_PER_BLOCK: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // 16  # = 4 for BLOCK_K=64
    57→```
    58→
    59→### LHS scale: `[BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]`
    60→
    61→```python
    62→# LHS (A): shape [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_K] — rows are M dimension
    63→scale_k_start = k_start // 16  # packed k-index / 16
    64→offs_scale_k = scale_k_start + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_BLOCK)
    65→a_scale = tl.load(
    66→    A_scale_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_as_m + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_as_k,
    67→    mask=mask_m[:, None],
    68→    other=0,
    69→)
    70→```
    71→
    72→### RHS scale: `[BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]` (N-first, NOT transposed)
    73→
    74→Even though B is stored transposed as `[K//2, N]`, the RHS scale must be **N-first**:
    75→
    76→```python
    77→# RHS (B): scale shape [BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_K] — N dimension is outer
    78→# B_scale stored as [N, K//32] (original layout, NOT transposed like B data)
    79→b_scale = tl.load(
    80→    B_scale_ptr + offs_n[:, None] * stride_bs_n + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_bs_k,
    81→    mask=mask_n[:, None],
    82→    other=0,
    83→)
    84→```
    85→
    86→**Error if RHS scale is transposed:** `rhs_scale must be a tensor of shape [64, 4]. Got ['4', '64']`
    87→
    88→## Kernel Template
    89→
    90→```python
    91→@triton.jit
    92→def _mxfp4_gemm_kernel(
    93→    A_ptr, A_scale_ptr,
    94→    B_ptr, B_scale_ptr,
    95→    C_ptr,
    96→    M, N, K_HALF,
    97→    stride_a_m, stride_a_k,
    98→    stride_as_m, stride_as_k,
    99→    stride_b_k, stride_b_n,
   100→    stride_bs_n, stride_bs_k,  # NOTE: N stride first for B_scale
   101→    stride_c_m, stride_c_n,
   102→    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr,
   103→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,
   104→    BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,  # in packed uint8 bytes (min 64)
   105→):
   106→    pid_m = tl.program_id(0)
   107→    pid_n = tl.program_id(1)
   108→    offs_m = pid_m * BLOCK_M + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_M)
   109→    offs_n = pid_n * BLOCK_N + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
   110→    mask_m = offs_m < M
   111→    mask_n = offs_n < N
   112→    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N], dtype=tl.float32)
   113→    SCALE_PER_BLOCK: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // 16
   114→
   115→    for k_start in range(0, K_HALF, BLOCK_K):
   116→        offs_k = k_start + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)
   117→        mask_k = offs_k < K_HALF
   118→
   119→        a = tl.load(A_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_a_m + offs_k[None, :] * stride_a_k,
   120→                    mask=mask_m[:, None] & mask_k[None, :], other=0)
   121→
   122→        scale_k_start = k_start // 16
   123→        offs_scale_k = scale_k_start + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_BLOCK)
   124→
   125→        # LHS scale: [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]
   126→        a_scale = tl.load(A_scale_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_as_m + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_as_k,
   127→                          mask=mask_m[:, None], other=0)
   128→
   129→        b = tl.load(B_ptr + offs_k[:, None] * stride_b_k + offs_n[None, :] * stride_b_n,
   130→                    mask=mask_k[:, None] & mask_n[None, :], other=0)
   131→
   132→        # RHS scale: [BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_BLOCK] — N-first
   133→        b_scale = tl.load(B_scale_ptr + offs_n[:, None] * stride_bs_n + offs_scale_k[None, :] * stride_bs_k,
   134→                          mask=mask_n[:, None], other=0)
   135→
   136→        acc = tl.dot_scaled(a, a_scale, "e2m1", b, b_scale, "e2m1", acc=acc)
   137→
   138→    c = acc.to(tl.bfloat16)
   139→    tl.store(C_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_c_m + offs_n[None, :] * stride_c_n, c,
   140→             mask=mask_m[:, None] & mask_n[None, :])
   141→```
   142→
   143→## Tensor Preparation (Python side)
   144→
   145→```python
   146→from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
   147→
   148→# Quantize A
   149→A_fp4, A_scale = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())
   150→A_scale = A_scale.view(torch.uint8)  # [M, K//32] uint8
   151→
   152→# Reuse B_q from input (matches dynamic_mxfp4_quant output exactly)
   153→# Only need to re-compute B_scale (B_q data is identical)
   154→_, B_scale = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(B.contiguous())
   155→B_scale = B_scale.view(torch.uint8)  # [N, K//32] uint8 — keep N-first
   156→
   157→# B data: transpose to [K//2, N] for K-major access
   158→B_fp4_t = B_q.view(torch.uint8).t().contiguous()
   159→
   160→# Block sizes
   161→BLOCK_M = max(16, min(64, triton.next_power_of_2(m)))  # min=16 mandatory
   162→BLOCK_N = min(64, triton.next_power_of_2(n))
   163→BLOCK_K = 64  # min=64 mandatory
   164→
   165→# Note: B_scale stays in [N, K//32] layout — strides are (stride_bs_n, stride_bs_k)
   166→# Pass strides as (B_scale.stride(0), B_scale.stride(1)) — N stride first
   167→_kernel[grid](
   168→    ...
   169→    B_scale.stride(0), B_scale.stride(1),  # stride_bs_n, stride_bs_k
   170→    ...
   171→)
   172→```
   173→
   174→## Error Messages → Cause
   175→
   176→| Error | Cause | Fix |
   177→|-------|-------|-----|
   178→| `lhs_scale must be tensor of shape [M, X]. Got ['M', 'full_K//16']` | Loading full K dimension of scale instead of per-tile | Load `SCALE_PER_BLOCK` entries per tile, not all at once |
   179→| `rhs_scale must be tensor of shape [N, X]. Got ['X', 'N']` | RHS scale transposed (K-first instead of N-first) | Keep B_scale in original [N, K//32] layout |
   180→| `Assertion 'inputVals.size() % 4 == 0'` | BLOCK_K < 64 | Set BLOCK_K = 64 |
   181→| Silent wrong results on smallest shape | BLOCK_M < 16 | Use `max(16, ...)` |
   182→
   183→## Context
   184→
   185→Discovered during Luma AMD Speedrun competition (March 2026) on AMD MI355X (gfx950),
   186→ROCm 7.1, Triton-ROCm 3.6.0, Torch 2.10+rocm7.1.
   187→
   188→The scale layout was reverse-engineered by reading error messages from the MI355X runner
   189→and cross-referencing with Helion-generated Triton code for MXFP4 kernels.
   190→
   191→Performance note: This Triton kernel achieves ~35 µs geomean vs aiter's ~24 µs for MXFP4 GEMM.
   192→The bottleneck WAS B scale re-quantization (~15 µs for `dynamic_mxfp4_quant(B)`).
   193→**SOLVED:** Use `e8m0_unshuffle` to reverse `e8m0_shuffle` and recover un-shuffled B_scale
   194→from `B_scale_sh` without re-quantizing. See `aiter-mxfp4-api-limitations` Limitation 11.
   195→B_q data can be reused directly from input (identical to dynamic_mxfp4_quant output).
   196→

Fix: pad to next power-of-2 and mask:\n\n```python\nQK_PAD: tl.constexpr = 1024  # next_pow2(576) = 1024\n\nq_offs = tl.arange(0, QK_PAD)             # 1024, not 576\nq_mask = q_offs < QK_DIM                  # mask out [576, 1024)\nq = tl.load(q_base + q_offs, mask=q_mask, other=0.0).to(tl.float32)\n\n# When loading 2D tiles, combine masks:\nkv_ptrs = kv_base + n_offs[:, None] * stride + q_offs[None, :]\nkv = tl.load(kv_ptrs, mask=kv_mask[:, None] & q_mask[None, :], other=0.0)\n```\n\n**Performance note:** Padding 576 → 1024 wastes 44% of register space and memory bandwidth.\nFor V accumulation (V_DIM=512, already power-of-2), no padding needed.\n\n## Error Messages → Cause\n\n| Error | Cause | Fix |\n|-------|-------|-----|\n| `lhs_scale must be tensor of shape [M, X]. Got ['M', 'full_K//16']` | Loading full K dimension of scale instead of per-tile | Load `SCALE_PER_BLOCK` entries per tile, not all at once |\n| `rhs_scale must be tensor of shape [N, X]. Got ['X', 'N']` | RHS scale transposed (K-first instead of N-first) | Keep B_scale in original [N, K//32] layout |\n| `Assertion 'inputVals.size() % 4 == 0'` | BLOCK_K < 64 | Set BLOCK_K = 64 |\n| Silent wrong results on smallest shape | BLOCK_M < 16 | Use `max(16, ...)` |\n| `ValueError: arange's range must be a power of 2` | Dimension (e.g. 576) is not power-of-2 | Pad to next power-of-2, mask extras |"
```json
}
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Two lessons captured that will prevent wasted submissions in future sessions:

1. **`tl.arange` power-of-2 error is a compile-time fail** — it triggers during JIT, not at load time, so you only discover it after waiting for the runner to accept the job and start compiling. Documenting the exact error message and fix saves one full submission cycle (~10 min).

2. **"Flash Attention for decode" sounds right but isn't** — the standard FA paper optimizes for *prefill* (long query sequences). Decode has qseqlen=1, which means the score computation is a vector-matrix product (GEMV), not a matrix-matrix product (GEMM). GPU matrix cores are designed for GEMM. This architectural mismatch is why our "correct" Triton kernel was 100x slower than a simple torch.einsum.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Two skills updated:

- **`amd-gfx950-tl-dot-scaled-constraints`** — Added `tl.arange` power-of-2 constraint with exact error message, fix (padding to 1024 + mask), and updated error table.

- **`deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap`** — Replaced the "kernel is written, ready to submit" section with confirmed performance data showing 9-127x slowdown, root cause (GEMV ≠ GEMM, no matrix core utilization), and a note on the Q-padding approach for future exploration.
